# Omnis 2.0 — Build Progress Tracker

> **Status:** Live build tracker for the Omnis 2.0 implementation.
> This document tracks what has been built, what is in progress, and what
> remains. It is the single source of truth for build progress.
>
> **References (read-only, never edited):**
>
> 1. [OMNIS_2_0_SPEC.md](OMNIS_2_0_SPEC.md) — product specification
> 2. [OMNIS_2_0_UI_SPEC.md](OMNIS_2_0_UI_SPEC.md) — UI/UX specification
> 3. [OMNIS_2_0_PLUGINS.md](OMNIS_2_0_PLUGINS.md) — plugin architecture & developer guide
> 4. [OMNIS_2_0_MUSICBEE_COMPARISON.md](OMNIS_2_0_MUSICBEE_COMPARISON.md) — a competitor feature-gap backlog (user-supplied, 2026-08-15). Additional candidate gaps once this table's own named items run thin — verify against real code before treating anything there as a confirmed gap, the same discipline every survey in this table's Build log already applies.

---

## Phase 1 — Reliability

| # | Item | Status |
| --- | ------ | -------- |
| 1 | Playback engine | 🟡 In progress — `AudioEngine` covers play/pause/seek/next/prev/gapless/crossfade/speed/pitch/skip-silence/shuffle/repeat/volume. Started §51.2's split (OS media-session integration → `playback_os_integration.dart`; A-B repeat → a decoupled, unit-tested `AbRepeatController`), shrinking the file from 1208 to ~880 lines. Also introduced `PlaybackEngine` (§51.3 capability interface) — `AudioEngine` now `implements` it, and `PlaybackWatchdog`/`PlaybackRecovery` depend on the interface, not the concrete class. Still owes the bigger `QueueController`/`OutputController`/`AudioSessionController` split — queue mutation and the crossfade state machine are still interleaved in the facade. Real-device smoke testing is now confirmed possible on Android (2026-08-13: a debug APK built, installed, and ran clean on an emulator — see `docs/BUILDING.md`'s "Real-device smoke testing" section) — Windows desktop specifically remains blocked here for a confirmed, specific reason (a Flutter-SDK/Visual-Studio-version CMake generator mismatch, also documented there), but that's no longer the whole story for "can this be smoke-tested at all." The bigger split is still deferred, now simply because it hasn't been done yet, not because testing is impossible. Saved/named A-B loops closed 2026-08-15 — MusicBee comparison §27 / spec §19's "saved loops" gap: confirmed genuinely unbuilt beforehand by reading `AbRepeatController` itself (`markA`/`markB`/`clear` is its entire API — `_loopA`/`_loopB` are two in-memory fields, nothing persisted, `clear()` or marking a new A discards the current loop permanently) and grepping both repos for `SavedLoop`/`LoopPreset`/`loop_store` — zero real hits. New `lib/core/ab_loop_store.dart` (`SavedAbLoop`, `AbLoopStore`) is the same atomic-write + schema-versioned-envelope template `CustomRadioStationStore` already established, deliberately with zero dependency on `AudioEngine`/`AbRepeatController` — pure storage, fully unit-tested standalone (11 tests in `test/ab_loop_store_test.dart`: save/reload, per-track filtering via `loopsForTrack`, delete, corrupt-JSON and single-bad-record tolerance, atomic-write, and an exact-millisecond round-trip check since `Duration` is stored as `startMs`/`endMs`). `AudioEngine` gained two thin wrappers — `saveCurrentLoop(name)` (reads `abRepeatRange`/`currentTrack`, a no-op returning `null` when either is absent) and `applyLoop(SavedAbLoop)` (`markLoopA`/`markLoopB` at the saved points) — neither has direct test coverage, since `AudioEngine` itself still has no test harness at all (item 9, confirmed still blocked on the injectable-player seam), the same gap every other `AudioEngine` method already shares. UI: `PlayerLayoutData` gained an `onLongPressAbRepeat` callback (threaded through every existing `PopupMenuButton`-free call site in `test/player_widgets_test.dart`/`player_layouts_test.dart`/`declarative_layout_test.dart`, none of which broke beyond needing the new required parameter added), `PlayerAbRepeatButton` (the single widget every layout already shares — confirmed only one instantiation site exists, so this one change reaches all six bundled layouts at once) wraps itself in a `GestureDetector` for long-press, and `now_playing_page.dart` gained a new `_SavedLoopsSheet` (a modal bottom sheet: "Save this loop" when a loop is actively looping, plus the current track's saved loops each with Apply/Delete). `now_playing_page.dart` has no widget-test harness at all (a pre-existing gap, same as `library_page.dart`'s own precedent already noted elsewhere in this log), so the UI wiring is `flutter analyze`-verified only. All new tests passed on the first real run. Full suite: 993 passing (was 982), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. |
| 2 | Queue | 🟡 In progress — manual queue (add/remove/reorder via `setQueue`/`addTrack`/`removeTrack`/`playAt`), shuffle, repeat. `AudioEngine.playNext()` (§7's "play next" vs "add to queue" distinction) is now genuinely reachable end-to-end (closed 2026-08-14): added to `PluginContext` (`packages/omnis_plugin_api`, `plugin-api-v0.14.0`) and `OmnisPluginContext`, and to the Library page's per-track menu ("Play next"/"Add to queue", alongside the existing "Edit tags"/"Rate track"/etc. entries — that menu previously had no queue actions at all). Queue history/snapshots closed 2026-08-15: new `lib/core/queue_history_store.dart` (`QueueHistoryEntry`/`QueueHistoryStore`) persists full `BaseTrack` snapshots per entry — not track ids resolved against the current library, since a queue commonly holds streaming/radio tracks that may never be in any scanned library at all, the same class of gap item 41's play-history fix closed for Recently Played earlier this session. Two kinds share one store, distinguished by whether `name` is set: an automatic rolling history (capped at `maxAutoHistoryEntries` = 20, oldest evicted first, a duplicate-in-a-row skipped rather than spamming a new entry for the same queue continuing) recorded via a `queueStream` listener wired in `MainCore` — observed from *outside* `AudioEngine` entirely, the same external-listener shape `PlaylistPage`'s own `queueStream` subscription already uses, so the engine itself gained no new dependency or persistence concern — and permanent, user-named snapshots (`PlaylistPage`'s "Current queue" detail view gained a "Save as snapshot" action) that auto-eviction never touches. New `lib/ui/queue_history_page.dart` (reached via a new "Queue history" card on the Playlists page) lists both, newest first, with Restore (`AudioEngine.setQueue` + `play`) and Delete per entry. Still no smart/rule-based continuation (mood/artist/album/genre/similar-track), queue rules, exclusions, or multiple queue sources — all separately-tracked, larger asks this increment didn't attempt. `ShuffleRepeatPlugin`'s de-clustering shuffle algorithm — real since before this session, but with zero test coverage until 2026-08-15 — got its first real tests, which found and fixed a genuine bug in its conflict-repair pass; see the same day's build-log entry. Queue cleanup closed 2026-08-15 (per `OMNIS_2_0_MUSICBEE_COMPARISON.md` §23): the Current Queue detail page's single "Save as snapshot" icon button became a "Queue actions" popup menu with four entries — "Save as snapshot" (unchanged), new "Save as playlist" (turns the live queue into a real persistent `Playlist` via the existing `PlaylistStore`, distinct from the ephemeral `QueueHistoryStore` snapshot path), new "Remove duplicates", and new "Clear played". Both new actions' index-selection logic lives in a new pure, dependency-free `lib/core/queue_operations.dart` (`QueueOperations.duplicateIndicesToRemove`/`playedIndicesToRemove`) deliberately kept free of `AudioEngine` so it's fully unit-tested (`test/queue_operations_test.dart`, 12 tests) without needing the still-deferred injectable-player test seam (item 9) — the UI layer just loops the returned indices through the already-solid `AudioEngine.removeTrack(index)`, always highest-index-first so earlier indices never shift out from under later removals. "Remove duplicates" keeps the first occurrence of each track id but never removes whatever's currently playing, even if it's itself a later duplicate. Queue reordering closed 2026-08-15 (per `OMNIS_2_0_MUSICBEE_COMPARISON.md` §23's "drag/drop reordering ... move to top, shuffle remaining" gap, confirmed genuinely unbuilt beforehand — grepped `lib/` for `Reorderable`/`onReorder`/`moveToTop`/`shuffleRemaining`, zero matches, and `AudioEngine` had no move/reorder method at all): new `AudioEngine.moveTrack(from, to)` and `AudioEngine.shuffleRemaining()`, both thin wrappers around new pure functions in `lib/core/queue_operations.dart` — `QueueOperations.reorder(queue, currentIndex, from, to)` (same `(oldIndex, newIndex)` convention `ReorderableListView.onReorder` already hands `_reorderPlaylist` elsewhere on this page, correctly re-deriving the moved-and/or-shifted `currentIndex` so the currently-playing track's identity is never lost, only its position) and `QueueOperations.shuffledRemaining(queue, currentIndex, {random})` (shuffles only what's after the current track, leaving history and the current track's position untouched; `currentIndex < 0` shuffles the whole queue). The Current Queue detail page's plain `ListView.builder` became a `ReorderableListView.builder` (drag-to-reorder, calling `_reorderQueue` → `AudioEngine.moveTrack`, with the same `OmnisHaptics.selectionClick()` feedback `_reorderPlaylist` already gives), each non-front row gained a "Move to top" trailing action, and the "Queue actions" popup menu (added in the cleanup-actions increment just above) gained a fifth "Shuffle remaining" entry. 12 new pure-logic tests in `test/queue_operations_test.dart` (reorder later/earlier/no-op-adjacent-drop/current-track-tracking/out-of-range-safety/new-list-not-mutated; shuffledRemaining boundary preserved/deterministic-with-seeded-Random/no-current-track/at-last-index/empty-queue) plus 7 new widget tests in `test/playlist_page_test.dart`'s new `Queue actions (item 2)` group wiring a real `_FakeEngine` (extended with a settable `fakeQueue`/`fakeCurrentIndex` and real `moveTrack`/`shuffleRemaining`/`removeTrack`/`playAt` implementations built on the same `QueueOperations` functions, rather than stubs) through the actual popup menu and per-row buttons — menu contents, remove-duplicates/clear-played/save-as-playlist end-to-end (the latter two had no prior widget coverage either), shuffle-remaining leaving the current track's identity and position alone, move-to-top actually reordering, and the currently-playing (front) track correctly having no move-to-top action at all. All passed on the first real run except two hand-derivation math bugs caught and fixed *before* writing the implementation (verified `reorder`'s index math on paper against Flutter's own documented `onReorder` off-by-one convention before coding, not just after). Full suite: 955 passing (was 936), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. Left deliberately undone (per the survey that recommended this increment): exposing `moveTrack`/`shuffleRemaining` through `PluginContext`/`OmnisPluginContext` for plugin-author parity with `playNext`/`removeTrack` — a `packages/omnis_plugin_api` change needing the full cross-repo version-bump cycle for a capability nothing currently asks for, deferred as a nice-to-have rather than a confirmed gap. Item 2's remaining named gaps: smart/rule-based continuation, queue rules/exclusions, multiple queue sources, queue groups, playlist-to-queue conversion beyond what already exists. |
| 3 | Recovery | ✅ Solid for Phase 1 — `PlaybackWatchdog` (stuck loading, position stall, impossible position, decoder exceptions, output loss) + `PlaybackRecovery` (reload → retry → advance → stop) + `RecoveryJournal` (crash/power-loss resume) are now wired end-to-end in `MainCore`: watchdog starts with the engine, counters reset on a healthy track start, journal saves on pause/track-change/20s-heartbeat, and a "Resume where you left off?" prompt offers restoration on launch. `RecoveryJournal.save()`/`clear()` also now serialize onto one queue — they're called from several independent unawaited sites, and two concurrent writes were racing on the same `.tmp` path. Untrusted-plugin isolation (isolates) is still future work per the plugin architecture doc. |
| 4 | Database | 🟡 In progress — still JSON-file storage, not an indexed DB. §41's "atomic writes" requirement is now real across every store that persists user-authored/derived data: `LibraryStore` (atomic + debounced, prior work), `RecoveryJournal` (atomic, this session), and `PlaylistStore`/`PlayHistoryStore` (atomic, this session) — all write to a sibling `.tmp` file and rename over the real path, so a crash mid-write can no longer corrupt/truncate any of them. §41 also calls for "corruption detection" — closed a related, more severe gap this session: `LibraryStore`/`PlaylistStore`/`PlayHistoryStore` all decoded their JSON as one bulk `.map(...).toList()`/`Map.map(...)`, so a *single* malformed record (one bad track, one bad playlist, one bad stats entry) threw, was caught by the outer try/catch, and silently wiped the *entire* store back to empty — for `PlaylistStore` specifically, that means losing every hand-curated playlist with nothing to regenerate them from. All three now decode entry-by-entry and skip only the bad one, matching the per-entry guard `PlaybackState.fromJson` already had for its queue. `RemoteTextStore` (theme/layout installs) deliberately left as-is — each install is one independent file, not shared mutable state, so the blast radius of an interrupted write is already contained. Added `lib/core/backup_service.dart` (§41/§48 "automatic backup... with validation before overwriting anything") — bundles library/playlists/play-history/recovery-journal into one zip with a manifest, and `restoreBackup()` validates the *entire* archive (manifest present + recognized version, every declared file present, every declared file's JSON syntactically valid) before writing a single byte — a bad backup touches nothing, never a partial apply. Deliberately scoped to just those four store files, not `AppSettings`/plugin credentials (that's closer to §47 "Universal export," a materially bigger, separate piece of work). Wired into Settings as a new "Backup" category (`lib/ui/settings/backup_settings_page.dart`), including the search index — "Backup Omnis"/"Restore Omnis" are now searchable and reachable from the running app, not just implemented in isolation. Restore refreshes `LibraryRepository`'s in-memory cache immediately; playlists/history/journal aren't cached the same way, so the UI is upfront that a restart is needed for those to fully take effect, rather than silently leaving stale in-memory state. The migration-system half of the original gap closed 2026-08-14: new `lib/core/schema_versioning.dart` — three small free functions (`wrapVersioned`/`unwrapVersioned`/`runMigrations`), not a class each store subclasses, matching how this session applied the atomic-write and per-entry-decode fixes above independently to each store rather than through one shared base type. Every store's JSON envelope is now `{"schemaVersion": N, "data": <the same payload shape each store already wrote}` instead of a bare array/object; an old on-disk file with no `schemaVersion` key decodes as version 0 with the whole file as its own payload — so nothing already written by a real install needs a one-time conversion pass, the next `save()` from any store upgrades it to the versioned envelope transparently. `runMigrations` walks a per-store `Map<int, SchemaMigration>` from the file's version up to that store's current one; every store's map is empty today (there's no real field/shape change to migrate yet, correctly, since this is each payload's first versioned release) but the dispatch mechanism itself is real and tested, not just a version *number* with nothing behind it. Wired into all four stores (`LibraryStore`, `PlaylistStore`, `PlayHistoryStore`, `RecoveryJournal`) with zero behavior change for any already-passing test. 19 new tests: 10 for the shared scaffold itself (`test/schema_versioning_test.dart` — wrap/unwrap round-trips, the legacy-bare-shape-decodes-as-v0 case, migrations running in order, a version with no registered migration being a no-op not an error, starting mid-chain only running remaining steps) plus 2-3 per store proving a real legacy file (the exact bare shape every on-disk file predates this scaffold with) still loads correctly and a fresh `save()` now writes the new envelope shape. Full suite: 721 passing (was 702), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. Still no multi-source libraries, no SQL-like query layer, no *real* payload migration yet (only the mechanism to run one). Automatic scheduled backups closed 2026-08-15 (`docs/OMNIS_2_0_SPEC.md`'s §50 line "Automatic scheduled backups," confirmed genuinely unbuilt beforehand: `BackupService` only ever exposed `createBackup()`/`restoreBackup()`, both invoked exclusively by the two manual buttons on `BackupSettingsPage`; grepping both repos for `autobackup`/`scheduled.*backup`/`backup.*schedul` returned zero hits, and `AppSettings` had no `autoBackup*` fields at all). New `lib/core/backup_scheduler.dart` (`BackupScheduler.isDue`/`filesToPrune`) is pure, dependency-free logic — `isDue(lastBackupAt, interval, now)` treats `null` (never run) as always due rather than making a fresh install/first-time-enable wait a full interval, and `filesToPrune` returns which of a set of existing files to delete to keep only the newest N, re-sorting defensively so a caller doesn't need to pre-sort. `AppSettings` gained `autoBackupEnabled` (default `false`, the same opt-in stance `highContrastEnabled`/`reduceMotionEnabled` already take for an unattended behavior change nobody asked for), `autoBackupIntervalDays` (default 7), and `lastAutoBackupAt` (nullable, stored as epoch-millis). `BackupService.maybeRunAutomaticBackup()` is the thin I/O wrapper: no-ops when disabled or not due, otherwise writes a zip straight to `<documents>/backups/omnis-backup-<timestamp>.zip` — no file-picker dialog, since this must run unattended, unlike `createBackup()` (which just returns bytes for the existing manual-save flow) — then prunes older automatic backups down to `autoBackupKeepCount` (5) via `BackupScheduler.filesToPrune`, then stamps `lastAutoBackupAt`. Never throws: a real failure (tested via an unwritable-path case — a plain file already sitting where the `backups/` subdirectory needs to go) is caught and swallowed rather than surfaced, the same "denial degrades, never blocks boot" contract `OmnisPermissions` already established. Wired into `MainCore.initialize()` as an unawaited fire-and-forget call at the very end of boot, right after plugin loading — writing a zip must never delay startup. `BackupSettingsPage` gained a new "Automatic backups" section (enable toggle, a Daily/Weekly/Monthly frequency `DropdownButton`, and a "Last automatic backup" row showing "Never yet" or the timestamp), only shown expanded once enabled. 12 new pure-logic tests in `test/backup_scheduler_test.dart` (due/not-due boundaries including the exact interval edge treated as due — `>=` not `>` — and a future-dated timestamp from clock skew correctly never being due; pruning's fewer/exact/more-than-keepCount cases, unsorted input, `keepCount` 0 and negative). 7 new tests in `test/backup_service_test.dart`'s new `maybeRunAutomaticBackup` group (disabled no-op, first-run-always-due, not-yet-due no-op, due-after-interval re-run, pruning beyond the keep count, and the unwritable-path failure case) — one real test-writing bug caught and fixed before it shipped, not a production bug: the pruning test initially asserted `keepCount + 1` files would remain, wrongly assuming the freshly-written backup was exempt from its own keep-count pool; the actual (correct) behavior keeps exactly `keepCount` total, since the new file is simply the newest entry in the same pool being pruned. 4 new widget tests in `test/backup_settings_page_test.dart` (off-by-default with the frequency picker hidden, enabling reveals it defaulting to Weekly/"Never yet", changing frequency persists) — needed a `tester.ensureVisible` before opening the `DropdownButton`, the same off-screen-popup-target lesson already documented elsewhere in this log for other pages' popup menus. All passed on the first real run except that one pruning-test fix. Full suite: 1014 passing (was 993), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. |
| 5 | Library scanning | 🟡 In progress — local-folder `MediaScanner` scan/rescan exists (pre-existing), with mtime-based incremental rescan and per-entry directory-walk error tolerance already in place. Fixed a gap this session: a file that vanished *after* being listed but *before* being stat'd (deleted mid-scan, removable storage unmounting) threw out of `file.lastModified()` and aborted the entire scan — now skipped like any other unreadable entry. Filesystem watchers closed 2026-08-15 — spec §8's "Industrial Library Scanner" section names it explicitly among its scanner feature list; confirmed genuinely unbuilt beforehand by grepping `lib/` for `watch(`/`FileSystemEvent`/`DirectoryWatcher` (only an unrelated `Stopwatch` hit) and confirming `MediaScanner.scanLibrary` was only ever called from one place — `library_page.dart`'s manual "Add audio files" rescan button — meaning rescanning was 100% user-initiated with nothing observing the filesystem in the background. New pure `lib/core/library_watcher.dart` (`LibraryWatcher`): wraps an injectable `Stream<FileSystemEvent> Function(String path)` (defaulting to real `Directory(path).watch(recursive: true)`) plus a debounce `Timer` — any event resets a 3-second quiet-period timer, so a batch copy/move (which fires one event per file) coalesces into exactly one rescan instead of one per file. A platform/sandbox where `Directory.watch` itself throws (some containers/restricted environments do) degrades to "not watching" rather than crashing, the same "denial degrades, never blocks boot" contract `OmnisPermissions` already follows. New `AppSettings.libraryWatcherEnabled` (default `false`, the same opt-in stance `autoBackupEnabled` already takes for unattended background work) and a "Watch folder for changes" toggle on the Library settings page, next to the existing folder picker. Wired into `MainCore.initialize()`/`dispose()` the same `Timer`-lifecycle shape `_journalTimer`/`_scheduleTimer` already have — started once from a settings snapshot at boot (desktop-only: Android already gets live results from `on_audio_query`'s own MediaStore index, so a filesystem watcher there would just be redundant background work; gated on `librarySource == LibrarySource.dedicatedFolder` with a real `selectedFolderPath` set). Deliberately not live-reactive to a settings change mid-session (toggling the setting or changing the watched folder takes effect on the next app restart, not immediately) — the same simplification this app's own volume/speed/crossfade settings already accept, restored once at startup rather than watched for live changes. The watcher's own rescan-on-settle callback mirrors `library_page.dart`'s existing `_pickAndAdd` merge logic exactly (add only genuinely new tracks, prune a local track whose file is now gone, leave every non-local track — Spotify/YouTube/radio/... — untouched) rather than naively replacing the whole library with a filesystem-only scan result, which would have silently dropped every non-local track. 8 new tests in `test/library_watcher_test.dart` using the `fake_async` package (already a dev dependency, previously unused) to control the debounce `Timer` deterministically: a single event fires after the quiet period, a burst of 20 events coalesces into exactly one rescan, nothing fires before the quiet period elapses, a second burst after the first settles fires a second rescan, `stop()` cancels a pending debounced rescan, `isWatching` reflects `start()`/`stop()` state, calling `start()` again replaces (not stacks) the previous watch, and a throwing watch function degrades to not-watching rather than propagating. 2 more in `test/library_settings_page_test.dart` (the new toggle renders/defaults off/persists, and its `highlightField` deep-link doesn't throw — deliberately never taps "Pick folder," the same real-native-dialog caution `backup_settings_page_test.dart` already documents) and 1 in `test/widget_test.dart` for the setting's own persistence. All passed on the first real run. Full suite: 1118 passing (was 1107), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. Still no scheduled scans (distinct from item 50's playback scheduling, which this doesn't attempt for scanning) or fingerprint-based identity — tracks are still identified primarily by path. Duplicate detection is already real and covered separately (see item 17's cleanup report), so it's no longer a gap here despite the spec listing it alongside the others. Scheduled scans closed 2026-08-16 — the scan-side analogue of item 4's "automatic scheduled backups" and item 29's "automatic plugin-update checking," both already closed the same way. New `lib/core/library_scan_scheduler.dart`: `LibraryScanScheduler.isDue(lastScanAt, interval, now)` (pure, identical `null`-is-always-due shape to `BackupScheduler.isDue`/`PluginUpdateScheduler.isDue`, kept as its own class per this codebase's established "one small scheduler per concern" convention rather than a shared generic one) plus a new pure `newTracksFromScan(current, scanned, {now})` — extracted, not duplicated, from `MainCore`'s pre-existing filesystem-watcher merge logic (the "which scanned tracks are genuinely new, stamped with a real `dateAdded` if the scanner didn't report one" half; the other half, pruning a track whose file has since disappeared, still needs real file-existence I/O and stays in `MainCore`). New `AppSettings.autoScanEnabled` (default `false`, the same opt-in stance `autoBackupEnabled`/`autoUpdateCheckEnabled` already take), `autoScanIntervalHours` (default 6 — more frequent than backup's daily-unit default since a rescan is read-mostly local work, no zip written, no network call), and `lastAutoScanAt`. New `MainCore._maybeRunScheduledScan()` — unlike the filesystem watcher (desktop-only, needs a real `Directory.watch`), this needs no platform/source gating of its own: `MediaScanner.scanLibrary` already branches to the right source (MediaStore on Android, filesystem walk elsewhere) and already no-ops on `LibrarySource.none`. Reuses the existing once-a-minute `_scheduleTimer` (already ticking for item 50's playback schedules) rather than adding a second `Timer`, plus one fire-and-forget call at boot — same "never block boot" contract `maybeRunAutomaticBackup`/`maybeCheckForUpdatesAutomatically` already follow, wrapped in try/catch so a scan failure can't crash the app. New "Scheduled scan" toggle + frequency `DropdownButton` + "Last scheduled scan" row on `lib/ui/settings/library_settings_page.dart`, directly below the existing "Watch folder for changes" toggle. 11 new tests in `test/library_scan_scheduler_test.dart` (isDue's boundary cases mirroring `backup_scheduler_test.dart`'s own; `newTracksFromScan`'s new-id detection, empty-scan/empty-library edges, dateAdded stamping vs. preserving an already-set one) and 3 new widget tests in `test/library_settings_page_test.dart` (toggle default/persistence with the frequency/last-run rows hidden until enabled, frequency selection persists, highlight-scroll). `MainCore`'s own new methods (`_maybeRunScheduledScan`/`_mergeScanIntoLibrary`) stay untested directly, the same standing gap every other `AudioEngine`/singleton-touching line in that file already has (item 9) — verified via `flutter analyze` plus the full suite staying green instead. All passed on the first real run — no bug found, a genuine feature-completion. Full suite: 1305 passing (was 1291), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. Item 5's one remaining named gap is fingerprint-based track identity. |
| 6 | Persistence | ✅ Solid for Phase 1 — playback state now survives crash/power-loss via `RecoveryJournal` (atomic writes, staleness cutoff); library and plugin-installer writes are atomic. Settings persistence (`AppSettings`) predates this session and is unchanged. |
| 7 | OS media integration | 🟡 In progress — Windows SMTC + `audio_service` notification/lock-screen controls exist (pre-existing). Not verified this session on macOS/Linux/mobile OS media session behavior. |
| 8 | Error handling | 🟡 In progress — decoder-error auto-skip, plugin-call sandboxing, plugin-installer download/zip-bomb size limits, a per-file scan failure no longer aborting the whole library scan, and (new) `library_page.dart`'s four `barrierDismissible: false` bulk-action dialogs (enrich, analyze, auto-tag, measure durations) can no longer be permanently stranded on screen — any exception mid-batch is now caught, the dialog is always dismissed, and (for `_measureDurations`) the "already running" guard flag always resets instead of silently disabling the feature for the rest of the session. Verified the metadata-enrichment and audio-analysis providers were already internally defensive (never throw) before treating this as a real gap — the actual bug was the dialogs' own lack of a `finally`, not the providers. The spec's full failure-mode checklist hasn't been run exhaustively against every subsystem — this is audit-as-you-go, not a completed sweep. |
| 9 | Tests | 🟡 In progress, meaningfully better — 478 tests passing (`flutter test`, `flutter analyze` clean; started this session at 433). New this session: `recovery_journal_test.dart`, a `MainCore` resumable-state group, `ab_repeat_controller_test.dart` (10 tests), and — via the new `PlaybackEngine` interface + `test/fakes/fake_playback_engine.dart` — `playback_watchdog_test.dart` (13 tests: every detection path) and `playback_recovery_test.dart` (10 tests: every failure-type branch, retry-then-advance behavior, custom retry limits). The recovery system, arguably the single most safety-critical subsystem per §43 of the spec, now has direct coverage for the first time. Remaining gap: `AudioEngine` itself (queue mutation, crossfade math beyond the pure `crossfadeVolumes` function, gapless/shuffle/repeat) is still untested — it needs either a real device/CI runner or an injectable-player seam of its own. |

> **Note on Phases 2–7 below:** this repo already had a mature Omnis app
> (library/playlist/settings pages, a plugin marketplace-style installer,
> a declarative theme/layout system, an accessibility pass, ~20 bundled
> plugins in `omnis_plugins`) before these Omnis 2.0 reference docs were
> added. The blanket "Not started" statuses below predate an actual
> audit against that existing code and should not be trusted as-is —
> most items likely have *some* pre-existing coverage. They need a
> dedicated audit pass (mapping existing code to each spec item) before
> being graded individually; that audit hasn't happened yet.

## Phase 2 — Library

> **Audited 2026-08-13** (Search deeply, the rest at a "does it exist and
> roughly how complete" pass) — the blanket "Not started" rows below were
> wrong for 7 of these 8 items; most of Phase 2 already had real,
> pre-existing implementations before this tracker existed.

| # | Item | Status |
| --- | ------ | -------- |
| 10 | Search | 🟡 In progress — **was a genuine 0% gap** (confirmed: zero matches for "search"/"query" anywhere in `library_page.dart` before this session), despite §6 calling search "one of Omnis' killer features." Added `lib/core/library_search.dart` (`filterTracks`) supporting free text across title/artist/album/genre plus `artist:`/`album:`/`genre:`/`title:`/`mood:`/`year:` (exact or `1990..1999` range) qualifiers, AND-combined across terms — wired into `library_page.dart`'s search box, filtering both the list and grid view paths and distinguishing "no results for this search" from "library is actually empty." `rating:` joined the list 2026-08-14 (exact or `>=`/`<=`/`>`/`<` comparison against `RatingsPlugin`'s 0-5 scale, 0 meaning unrated) — see item 15 for the design note on why this stayed a plugin-free pure function rather than growing a dependency. Quoted multi-word values joined the list 2026-08-14: `album:"greatest hits"` now matches that exact phrase (a bare, unquoted `album:greatest hits` still splits into two independent AND'd terms — `album:greatest` plus the free-text word `hits` — the original, still-intentional behavior; quoting exists to let a caller opt into a tighter phrase match instead of that looser default). Quoting works unqualified too (`"exact phrase"` as one free-text term). Fixed with a small tokenizer (`_tokenize`) that keeps a `"..."` span intact — including any whitespace inside it — instead of the previous plain `split(RegExp(r'\s+'))`, which is what broke a multi-word quoted value apart in the first place; `_SearchTerm.parse`'s own field-vs-value regex needed no change at all, since it already accepted a value containing spaces once the tokenizer stopped splitting on them first. An unterminated quote (`artist:"queen`) extends to the end of the query rather than erroring — the same forgiving convention most search boxes use — and an empty quoted value (`artist:""`) leaves nothing after the `:`, so it degrades to a plain free-text word rather than a field term, consistent with how any other field-looking prefix with no value already behaved. 8 new tests in `test/library_search_test.dart` (was 28, now 36), including one that deliberately builds a fixture where a track's album contains "greatest" and "hits" as separate words but not as the contiguous phrase, to prove quoting actually narrows the match rather than merely happening to still include the same tracks. Full suite: 666 passing (was 658), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. `bpm:`/`format:`/`favorite:` closed 2026-08-15: `bpm:` (exact/range/comparison, reading `BaseTrack.bpm` directly — no plugin lookup needed) and `format:` (exact case-insensitive match against `BaseTrack.codec`, categorical not substring — `format:mp` deliberately does not match "MP3") need no caller-supplied lookup at all, since both live directly on `BaseTrack`; `favorite:true`/`favorite:false` needed one, mirroring `rating:`'s `ratingOf` pattern exactly — `library_page.dart` passes its existing `_isFavorite` (backed by `FavoritesPlugin.isFavorite`), reusing a method already used elsewhere on that page rather than introducing a new one. 12 new tests in `test/library_search_test.dart`, plus fixed one pre-existing test whose own premise depended on `format:` staying unrecognized (it used `format:flac` as its example of an unknown-field-falls-back-to-free-text case — switched to `bitrate:1000`, which is still genuinely unsupported). Full suite: 891 passing (was 879), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. `bitrate:`/`lyrics:` closed 2026-08-15, same day: `bitrate:` (exact/range/comparison against `BaseTrack.bitrateKbps`, same shape as `bpm:`, no lookup needed) and `lyrics:true`/`lyrics:false` (needed a lookup, but keyed by the whole `BaseTrack` rather than an id — `LyricsPlugin.hasLyrics(BaseTrack)`'s own real signature — unlike `ratingOf`/`favoriteOf`, which are id-keyed since `RatingsPlugin`/`FavoritesPlugin` only ever need an id; `library_page.dart` passes `_lyricsPlugin?.hasLyrics` straight through as a bound-method tear-off). Also renamed the shared `favorite:`/`lyrics:` boolean-value parser from `_matchesFavorite` to `_matchesBoolean` once it started serving two different fields. 8 new tests in `test/library_search_test.dart`; had to fix the "unrecognized field falls back to free text" test a *second* time in the same day — its example value (`bitrate:1000`, itself a fix for the same test's earlier `format:flac` example going stale) went stale again the moment `bitrate:` became real, so it now uses `missing:artwork` (confirmed still genuinely unsupported, and unlikely to become a supported *boolean* field later since it's actually planned as a multi-value sub-field like `missing:artwork`/`missing:year`, a different shape). Full suite: 899 passing (was 891), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. `missing:`/`duplicate:` closed 2026-08-16, the last two named qualifiers: `missing:artwork`/`missing:year`/`missing:bpm`/`missing:genre` are per-track checks reading straight off `BaseTrack` (`coverArt`/`year`/`bpm`/`genres`), no caller-supplied lookup needed — the first genuinely multi-value sub-field this qualifier set has, unlike every prior boolean/numeric/string field. `duplicate:track`/`duplicate:album` are the first *library-wide* qualifiers: `filterTracks` now precomputes two `Set<String>` of duplicate track ids (title+primary-artist grouping) and duplicate-album track ids (album+primary-artist, two-tier raw-then-normalized grouping so two different artists' same-named albums are never conflated) — lazily, only when a query actually contains `duplicate:track`/`duplicate:album`, since it's an O(n) pass over the whole library rather than a per-track lookup. Both groupings independently reimplement (rather than import) the same matching logic `LibraryCleanupAnalyzer._duplicateTracks`/`_duplicateAlbums` already use, since those are private to that file and shaped to return grouped tracks for a report UI, not a flat id set a per-track predicate needs. `_SearchTerm.matches` gained two new `Set<String>` params to carry these down from `filterTracks`'s one-time computation to every term's per-track check. 13 new tests in `test/library_search_test.dart` (now 69 total in the file): each `missing:` sub-field, an unrecognized sub-field of either kind matching nothing rather than throwing, `duplicate:track`/`duplicate:album` as genuinely independent groupings (same tracks flagged as duplicate by title+artist but not by album+artist, and vice versa), a blank album never counting as a "duplicate" blank-album match, and both qualifiers composing with other terms via AND. Also fixed the "unrecognized field falls back to free text" test's worked example a third time — `missing:artwork` (the previous entry's replacement for `bitrate:1000`) stopped being unsupported the moment this increment shipped, so it now uses `wishlist:someday`, a field name with no planned real meaning in this app. Full suite: 1131 passing (was 1118), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed — `filterTracks`'s own public signature (params `tracks`/`query`/`ratingOf`/`favoriteOf`/`hasLyrics`) is unchanged, so `library_page.dart`'s one call site needed no edit at all. Item 10's full named search-qualifier list is now closed; still open: natural-language queries and search scope beyond the Library page (no global Ctrl+K command palette per items 10/45/48). |

| 12 | Artwork | 🟡 Pre-existing, real — `BaseTrack.coverArt`, Android `mediastore://` artwork via `MediaScanner`, embedded-artwork extraction in `TagEditorPlugin`, a dedicated `ArtistImagePlugin`, and a `TrackArtwork` widget used throughout the UI. Manual/drag-drop artwork override was already real by the time this was next surveyed — `lib/ui/tag_editor_dialog.dart`'s `_pickArtwork()` (`file_picker` → `TagEditorPlugin.writeTags(artworkBytes: ...)`) predates this entry's own last update, so that half of the old wording was stale, not an open gap; only the online-lookup half was genuine. Cover Art Archive online lookup closed 2026-08-15 (spec §47's own italic annotation names this item explicitly): confirmed the gap by grepping both repos for `coverartarchive`/`fanart.tv`/`cover art archive` — zero hits anywhere. `Omnis-Plugins/lib/metadata_enrichment_plugin.dart`'s existing MusicBrainz recording query already resolved a matched *release* (title/date/artist) but discarded `release['id']` (the release MBID) — exactly the id Cover Art Archive keys artwork by. New `Omnis-Plugins/lib/cover_art_archive.dart` (`CoverArtArchive.frontCoverUrl`/`fetchFrontCover`) is a small, dependency-light module kept separate from `metadata_enrichment_plugin.dart` so the URL-building logic — the part worth unit-testing without a network — stays free of the HTTP client/`PluginStorage`/every other concern that file already carries; a 404 (a release with no archived art, common and expected) degrades to `null`, not an error, the same "don't manufacture a claim the data can't support" stance every other lookup-style feature in this codebase already takes. `_MusicBrainzMatch` gained a `releaseMbid` field (previously silently dropped) and a new `MetadataEnrichmentPlugin.lookupArtwork(track)` chains the existing MusicBrainz query into the new archive fetch — `null` when the track can't be matched, the matched release has no MBID, or the archive has nothing for it. Wired into `library_page.dart` as a new "Look up artwork online" per-track menu action (single-track only this pass, not a bulk "whole library" action — deliberately scoped narrower than `_enrichAll`'s rate-limited batch flow, a real but separate follow-on), reusing `TagEditorPlugin.writeTags(artworkBytes: ...)` — the exact same call the manual file-picker flow (`TagEditorDialog._pickArtwork`) already makes — followed by `ArtworkProvider.invalidate(track.id)` so the next render picks up the new embedded art instead of a stale cached miss. 13 new tests: 8 in `Omnis-Plugins/test/cover_art_archive_test.dart` (URL building with/without a size suffix, a 200 returning real bytes, a 404/other-non-200/thrown-exception all degrading to `null`, the mbid passed through verbatim) and 5 in `metadata_enrichment_plugin_test.dart`'s new `lookupArtwork` group (the full MusicBrainz→archive chain via a `MockClient`, no-release-match, matched-release-with-no-mbid never even calling the archive, a 404 from the archive, and the existing empty-artist/title short-circuit). `Omnis-Plugins` suite: 448 passing (was 435), tagged `v0.29.0`. Cross-repo version bump: this repo's `pubspec.yaml` `omnis_plugins` ref bumped from `v0.28.0` to `v0.29.0` (no `omnis_plugin_api` change needed this time — `_MusicBrainzMatch` is private and `IMetadataProvider` itself was untouched), clean-clone resolution re-verified. All new tests passed on the first real run. `library_page.dart`'s new menu action has no widget-level test — same standing precedent as `_rateTrack`/`_setThumb`/every other action on that page's menu (no widget-test harness exists for this file at all), `flutter analyze`-verified only. Main-repo full suite: 1083 passing (unchanged — this increment adds no main-repo tests, only `Omnis-Plugins` ones), `flutter analyze` clean in both repos. Still open: no bulk "look up artwork for the whole library" action, no `IArtworkProvider` capability interface (only one provider exists so far, the same "add the interface once a second consumer needs it" restraint `IRatingsProvider`/`IFavoritesProvider` were introduced under), and no Fanart.tv as a second source. Bulk "look up artwork for the whole library" closed 2026-08-16 — this entry's own text already named it as the deliberately-deferred follow-on to the single-track lookup above. New pure `lib/core/artwork_candidates.dart` (`tracksNeedingArtwork(tracks)`) filters to local tracks with a real `localPath` and no real `coverArt` (a blank/whitespace-only string still counts as missing, not "has artwork") — the same "don't manufacture/attempt work that isn't needed" filtering style `library_cleanup_analyzer.dart`'s own categories already use. New `LibraryPage._lookupArtworkForAll()` mirrors `_enrichAll()`'s exact shape field-for-field (confirmation dialog naming the real candidate count and MusicBrainz's ~1 req/sec rate limit, a cancellable `barrierDismissible: false` progress dialog, the same try/catch/finally-must-always-dismiss discipline) but only loops over `tracksNeedingArtwork(_tracks)` rather than the whole library, reusing the exact `MetadataEnrichmentPlugin.lookupArtwork` → `TagEditorPlugin.writeTags(artworkBytes: ...)` → `ArtworkProvider.invalidate` sequence `_lookupArtworkOnline` already established for one track — a real but genuinely separate action from `_enrichAll`'s metadata-only batch flow, not a duplicate. New "Look up artwork for the whole library" entry in the Library page's tools menu, alongside "Look up metadata for the whole library." 7 new tests in `test/artwork_candidates_test.dart` (empty library, a real candidate, a blank-string coverArt still counting as missing, already-has-artwork excluded, no-localPath excluded, non-local-track excluded even with no coverArt, a mixed library returning only the genuinely-needed one). All passed on the first real run — no bug found, a genuine feature-completion. `LibraryPage`'s own UI wiring has no widget-level test, same standing precedent as `_lookupArtworkOnline`/`_enrichAll`/every other action on that page. | 13 | Playlists | 🟡 Pre-existing, real and fairly complete — `PlaylistStore` (static playlists, atomic + per-entry-safe writes, both hardened this session), M3U/M3U8 import/export, and (as of 2026-08-14, see item 42) a real rule-based `SmartPlaylistPlugin` engine alongside its pre-existing flat mood matcher. Folders/groups closed 2026-08-15: new `lib/core/playlist_folder_store.dart` (`PlaylistFolder`, `PlaylistFolderStore`) persists a flat (one-level, no nesting) set of named folders plus a `playlistId -> folderId` assignment map, in its own atomic-write + schema-versioned file — deliberately kept out of the shared `Playlist` model in `packages/omnis_plugin_api` (which `Omnis-Plugins` also depends on via git) since folder organization is a purely local UI concern no plugin needs to know about, and adding a field there would mean a cross-repo version bump for no plugin-facing benefit. `PlaylistPage` groups playlists under their folder (with an "Unsorted" section for the rest, shown only once at least one folder exists — a user who's never created one sees the exact same flat list this page always rendered), with create/rename/delete-folder actions and a per-playlist "Move to folder…" picker; deleting a folder un-assigns its playlists rather than deleting them, and deleting a playlist cleans up its own now-dangling assignment entry. PLS import/export closed 2026-08-15: `PlaylistStore.exportPLS`/`importPLS` mirror the existing M3U pair exactly (same local-tracks-only export rule, same path-then-filename-fallback import matching), producing/reading the standard `[playlist]`/`FileN`/`TitleN`/`LengthN`/`NumberOfEntries`/`Version=2` shape; `playlist_page.dart`'s import action became a format-choice popup menu (M3U/PLS) and both playlist menus (list tile + detail view) gained an "Export as PLS" item alongside the existing M3U one. XSPF import/export closed 2026-08-15: `PlaylistStore.exportXSPF`/`importXSPF` are a third parallel pair alongside M3U/PLS (same local-tracks-only export rule, same path-then-filename-fallback import matching), hand-writing/regex-extracting the XSPF XML shape directly rather than adding an XML package dependency for a handful of fixed elements — `<location>` (a real `file://` URI via `Uri.file`, XML-escaped/unescaped on export/import), `<title>`/`<creator>`/`<duration>` (milliseconds) on export, only `<location>` read back on import (matching PLS's own "only read what's needed to resolve a track" stance). `playlist_page.dart`'s import menu and both playlist export menus gained a third XSPF entry. CSV/JSON export closed 2026-08-15, MusicBee-comparison §46's "CSV/JSON export" gap: confirmed genuinely unbuilt beforehand (grepped `lib/` for `exportCSV`/`exportJson`/`exportJSON`/`toCsv` — zero matches; `playlist_store.dart` had exactly the M3U/PLS/XSPF trio, no fourth). Unlike those three — playback formats a real media player has to open, so only a local track with a real file path is representable — `PlaylistStore.exportCSV`/`exportJSON` are spreadsheet/interchange exports and deliberately include **every** resolved track regardless of type (a Spotify/YouTube/radio entry is real playlist data worth exporting even though nothing here can play it back from the file); a track id no longer in the library is still skipped, since there's genuinely no data left for it. `exportCSV` writes a header row (Title/Artist/Album/Album Artist/Genre/Year/Track Number/Disc Number/Duration/Type/Local Path) plus one row per track, with a small `_csvEscape` helper doing real RFC 4180 quoting (comma/quote/newline triggers a quoted field with doubled internal quotes) rather than assuming clean input. `exportJSON` writes a top-level object (playlist name, `createdAt`, a `tracks` array of per-track objects) via `JsonEncoder.withIndent`. No import counterpart for either — the comparison doc only asks for export, and CSV/JSON aren't playback formats a "restore my playlist" flow would round-trip through the way M3U/PLS/XSPF are designed to. `playlist_page.dart`'s two export popup menus (list-tile row + detail-view AppBar) each gained "Export as CSV"/"Export as JSON" entries alongside the existing three, via new `_exportPlaylistCsv`/`_exportPlaylistJson` methods mirroring `_exportPlaylistXspf`'s exact `FilePicker.saveFile` structure. 8 new tests in `test/playlist_store_test.dart`'s new `CSV/JSON export (item 13)` group (header+row shape including a non-local track, RFC 4180 comma/quote escaping, a plain field staying unquoted, an empty playlist still producing just the header row for CSV / an empty `tracks` array for JSON, and the JSON shape's `playlist`/`createdAt`/per-track fields) plus 2 new widget tests in `test/playlist_page_test.dart`'s new `CSV/JSON playlist export (item 13, §46)` group confirming both menu entries actually appear in both menus alongside the pre-existing three — this repo's export menus had **no prior widget-test coverage at all** (confirmed by grepping for "Export as" in that test file before adding these), so this is also the first UI-level check that any of the five export formats are genuinely reachable from the menu, not just that the underlying store method works. All passed on the first real run — no bug found. Full suite: 982 passing (was 973), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. Collaborative playlists remains the one named gap left for this item; MusicBee-comparison §46's own remaining half (MusicBee/Plex/Jellyfin-native export formats) is a separate, larger, format-specific undertaking not attempted here. |

Wired into `library_page.dart`: a "Rate track" menu item opens a 5-star picker, and a compact star indicator appears on any row that's actually rated (mirroring `FavoritesPlugin`'s heart-icon integration exactly). `ratedAtLeast()` exists as the building block for a `rating:>=4`-style search operator — wired 2026-08-14: `filterTracks` gained an optional `ratingOf` lookup parameter (the caller supplies it — `library_page.dart` passes `RatingsPlugin.ratingOf` — rather than the function growing a plugin dependency itself, keeping it pure and independently testable) plus a `rating:` field supporting exact and `>=`/`<=`/`>`/`<` comparisons against the 0-5 scale (0 = unrated, matching `RatingsPlugin.ratingOf`'s own convention). New `ThumbState { none, up, down }` enum; `thumbOf`/`setThumb` follow the exact "tap the active value again to clear" convention `setRating` already established. `SmartPlaylistRule` gained `RuleField.thumbUp`/`thumbDown` (equals-only, boolean-style, threaded through `RuleCondition.matches`/`SmartPlaylistRule.apply`/`SmartPlaylistPlugin.buildQueueForRule` exactly like `favorite:` — the builder UI's boolean-field value-swap logic, previously `favorite`-specific, generalized to `_isBooleanField`/`_booleanLabels` covering all three fields with field-specific dropdown wording, e.g. "Thumbed up"/"Not thumbed up"). `library_page.dart`: a thumb icon (only shown once set, same "don't clutter every row" reasoning the star rating already uses) plus "Thumbs up"/"Thumbs down" per-track menu items, tapping the currently-active one clears it. `RatingsPlugin`'s own settings page's "Clear all" now wipes both signals together and its confirmation/count text says so honestly rather than silently). 15 new tests in `Omnis-Plugins/test/ratings_plugin_test.dart` (persistence, overwrite between up/down, clearing, independence from star ratings, per-track isolation, `thumbCount`, `clearAll` wiping both, corruption resilience — mirroring every existing ratings test), 8 in `smart_playlist_rule_test.dart` (equals-true/false matching for both fields, non-equals-operator/no-lookup-supplied safety, ALL/ANY/NONE composition), 4 widget tests in `smart_playlist_plugin_edit_test.dart` (field-swap UI, saving both default and non-default values, field-specific labels), and 2 real cross-plugin `PluginManager`/`ServiceRegistry` integration tests in this repo's `test/smart_playlist_ratings_integration_test.dart` (a `thumbUp:` condition matching through the real registered `IThumbsProvider`; disabling `RatingsPlugin` unregistering `IThumbsProvider` too, proving both interfaces share one enable/disable lifecycle) — `Omnis-Plugins` suite: 435 passing (was 406), tagged `v0.28.0`. Calculated album/artist ratings closed 2026-08-16 — MusicBee comparison §36's remaining "album/artist-level ratings, a distinction between user rating and a calculated one" fragment (half-stars, the other item named in that same sentence, is a separate, still-open gap — a genuine 6-value display precision change to the star-picker UI itself, not attempted here). Confirmed genuinely unbuilt by grepping both repos for `albumRating`/`artistRating`/`averageRating` — zero hits outside the comparison doc; `RatingsPlugin` only ever stores a per-track rating, and neither the Albums nor Artists grouped view in `library_page.dart` showed any rating info at all. New pure `lib/core/rating_aggregation.dart`: `averageRating(tracks, ratingOf)` averages only the rated tracks in a group (rating `> 0`, the same "0 means unrated" convention `RatingsPlugin.ratingOf`/`rating:0` already use), returning `null` — not a misleading "0.0" — when nothing in the group has been rated at all, the same "don't manufacture a claim the data can't support" stance `library_cleanup_analyzer.dart`/`library_statistics.dart` already take for their own aggregate stats. This average is never persisted anywhere — recomputed fresh from live per-track ratings every time, which is the actual "user rating vs. calculated one" distinction the comparison doc asks for. `LibrarySection` (`library_page.dart`) gained an `allTracks` getter (recursive: a nested section like an Artists-view artist row has an empty `tracks` list of its own, with every real track living under its album/track children — `allTracks` flattens that whole subtree, which `tracks` alone can't). Wired in three places: the Artists-view `ExpansionTile`'s subtitle (top-level artist rows only, via `allTracks`), the Albums-view flat section header, and both view modes' grid tiles — rendered as "★ 4.2 (12 rated)," shown only when at least one member track has a real rating. Deliberately scoped to Albums/Artists only, not Songs (which already shows each track's own star) or Genres (the survey that found this gap didn't flag it as commonly expected there, and the comparison doc's own wording only names album/artist). 7 new tests in `test/rating_aggregation_test.dart` (empty list, no-ratings-at-all, single rating, unrated tracks excluded from both the average and the count, uniform ratings, a fractional average, a three-way average) and 2 in `test/library_grouping_test.dart` for the new `allTracks` getter (flattening a real nested artist/album/track structure; a no-op on an already-flat section). All passed on the first real run — no bug found, a genuine feature-completion. `library_page.dart`'s own UI wiring has no widget-level test, same standing precedent as `_rateTrack`/`_toggleFavorite`/every other action on that page. Undo/backup/restore closed 2026-08-14: `writeTags` now snapshots the pre-write tag field values (decoded from the file bytes already read into memory for the write itself, not a second disk read) whenever a write succeeds, keyed by file path — a small JSON blob per file rather than a full-file byte backup, since a whole-file copy per edited track wouldn't scale to a library-wide batch operation the way it reasonably can for a single plugin's own install directory (item 29's `backupPluginDirectory`). Two real bugs found and fixed purely by writing tests against a real temp ID3 file, not by inspection: (1) restoring a field to `null` (an edit that *added* a title/artist/album where none existed before) silently left the edited value in place instead of clearing it, since `writeTags` treats `null` as "don't touch this field" for a `TXXX` custom field — but title/artist/album are *always* passed through to the encoder regardless of null-ness, so an empty string (not `null`) is what actually clears them, and the fix uses that distinction specifically for those three fields; (2) an early version explicitly deleted the undo snapshot after a successful restore using a stale in-memory copy read *before* that restore's own `writeTags` call ran — silently erasing the fresh snapshot that inner call had itself just correctly saved, the exact "lost update from a stale read" bug class this session already fixed in `LibraryStore`/`PlayHistoryStore` earlier today, recreated here by hand and caught the same way: a real failing test, not code review. Deliberately keeps only the *most recent* snapshot per file (undoing twice toggles between the last two states, not an arbitrary history), and deliberately does **not** clear the snapshot after a successful undo — the restore's own `writeTags` call naturally creates a fresh one for the just-undone state, which is what makes a second `undoLastEdit` call toggle back to it instead of dead-ending. 7 new tests in `test/tag_editor_plugin_test.dart` (in the main repo, matching where this plugin's file-round-trip tests already lived — `Omnis-Plugins`' own test file explicitly defers that coverage there). `Omnis-Plugins` suite: 235 passing (unchanged), tagged `v0.19.0`. Confirmed genuinely open: `TagEditorPlugin` (`Omnis-Plugins`) already reads/writes a real composer tag (`TCOM`/`TXXX:COMPOSER` via `TrackTags.composer`) and `TagEditorDialog` already exposes a "Composer" edit field, but `BaseTrack` had no field to carry it — composer was edit-only, never scanned into the library model, never searchable, never displayable. New nullable `BaseTrack.composer` (`packages/omnis_plugin_api`, the standard additive-field shape: constructor/`copyWith`/`toJson`/`fromJson` with absent-decodes-as-null backward compat/`==`/`hashCode`) — a genuine cross-repo contract change, tagged `plugin-api-v0.19.0`. `lib/core/media_scanner.dart`'s `_trackFromFile` now threads `tags.composer` into the scanned `BaseTrack` — "picked up for free from the same tag read already happening," the identical shape `replayGain`/`albumArtist` were wired in with. New `composer:` search qualifier in `lib/core/library_search.dart`, substring match, same shape as `album:`/`title:` (deliberately not part of bare free-text matching, matching every other qualified-only field here). **A real, non-trivial cross-repo consequence discovered and handled, not assumed away**: bumping `plugin-api-v0.19.0` alone was not sufficient to resolve — `Omnis-Plugins`' own `omnis_plugins` git dependency is pinned to a *tagged* release (`v0.36.0`) whose own frozen `pubspec.yaml` still referenced the old `plugin-api-v0.18.0`; pub's git-dependency resolution requires exact ref agreement on both edges, so `flutter pub get` failed with a clear "forbidden" conflict until `Omnis-Plugins`' `pubspec.yaml` was ALSO bumped to `plugin-api-v0.19.0` and a fresh `omnis_plugins` tag (`v0.37.0`) cut on that commit — only then did the main repo's own `omnis_plugins` ref bump to `v0.37.0` resolve cleanly. 6 new tests in `test/base_track_test.dart` (default-null, JSON round-trip, legacy-JSON-decodes-as-null backward compat, `copyWith`, equality/hashCode both distinguishing different composers and matching identical ones) and 3 new tests in `test/library_search_test.dart`'s new composer-field group (substring case-insensitive match, no-composer-never-matches, confirming `composer:` is deliberately excluded from bare free-text matching). All passed on the first real run — no bug found, a genuine feature-completion. `Omnis-Plugins` suite: 525 passing (unchanged — no code change there, ref bump only), tagged `v0.37.0`. Main-repo full suite: 1361 passing (was 1352), `flutter analyze` clean in both repos. |

## Phase 3 — Audio

> **Audited 2026-08-13.** Full findings in [OMNIS_2_0_MISSED_DEEP_PHASE.md](OMNIS_2_0_MISSED_DEEP_PHASE.md#phase-3--audio).

| # | Item | Status |
| --- | ------ | -------- |
| 18 | DSP pipeline | 🟡 Partial — a flat named-multiplier gain-composition system (`AudioEngine.setGainContribution`), not the spec's staged, independently-replaceable chain. Zero compressor/limiter/crossfeed/convolver/spatializer/room-correction anywhere in either repo. |
| 19 | ReplayGain | 🟡 Partial — `ReplayGainPlugin` applies gain from tags already written by an external tool (foobar2000 etc.) plus a user preamp; Omnis never scans/computes ReplayGain itself. Album-gain mode closed 2026-08-15: a persisted toggle (default off, prior behavior unchanged) prefers `ReplayGainValues.albumGain` over `trackGain` — data `TagEditorPlugin` was already parsing from file tags into `BaseTrack.replayGain` but that `setReplayGain` never read — falling back to track gain for a track with no album gain tag rather than leaving it unnormalized. Still 0% on Omnis computing ReplayGain itself. |
| 20 | EQ | 🟡 Partial — real Android hardware EQ + a 3-band virtual trim everywhere else, per-*device* profiles (not per-artist/album), no parametric mode, no selectable band count. Per-artist/per-album profiles closed 2026-08-16 — the "not per-artist/album" half of that gap. `EqualizerPlugin.onTrackStart` was a stubbed no-op, a strong pre-existing signal this was the intended hook; confirmed genuinely unbuilt by reading the whole file first. New "Save for [Artist]"/"Save for [Album]" buttons in the bands editor persist the current bands under that specific artist/album (new `_virtualKeyForArtist`/`_virtualKeyForAlbum`/`_hardwareKeyForArtist`/`_hardwareKeyForAlbum`, the same device-keying string-suffix shape `_virtualKeyFor`/`_hardwareKeyFor` already use); `onTrackStart` now resolves which saved profile actually applies via a new pure, static `_resolveKey({candidates, fallback, hasProfile})` — the first candidate key an injected `hasProfile` predicate confirms is actually saved, or the existing device/shared-default key if none are, kept dependency-free so precedence logic never needs a real `PluginStorage` to exercise. Precedence is deliberately **album beats artist** (a saved album profile is the more specific choice — a user who bothered to save one for one particular album by an artist clearly wants it to override whatever they saved for that artist more broadly), then device, then the shared default — a judgment call made and documented explicitly, not just implemented silently. A new `_activeVirtualKey`/`_activeHardwareKey` pair tracks which key bands were actually loaded from, so a plain slider drag or Reset (via the existing `persistVirtualBands`/`persistHardwareBands`) updates *that* profile — the one currently in effect, which might now be an artist/album one — rather than always forcing writes back to the device/default key regardless of what's active, a real behavioral question this increment had to resolve, not just an extension of existing plumbing. 8 new tests in `test/equalizer_plugin_test.dart`'s new `per-artist/per-album profiles` group (artist/album exposed from a real `onTrackStart`, no-ops with nothing playing yet, an artist profile applying across a different album by the same artist, album beating artist for the same track and falling back to the artist profile for a different album, artist/album beating a connected device's own profile — verified via a genuine re-resolve on a second `onTrackStart`, not just reading back an in-memory value `setBand` had just set, catching that exact test-writing shortcut before it shipped — plain edits updating the now-active profile, and a blank artist/album never producing a profile key at all) — one real test-writing bug caught and fixed before it shipped, not a production bug: an early draft of the "auto-applies for another track by the same artist" test asserted a fresh track with no artist/album profile yet falls back to *flat* rather than the already-saved shared default profile, forgetting that `_resolveKey`'s fallback IS that shared/device key, not nothing. All passed after that one fix. `Omnis-Plugins` suite: 525 passing (was 517), tagged `v0.36.0`, pushed. Cross-repo version bump: this repo's `pubspec.yaml` `omnis_plugins` ref bumped `v0.35.0` → `v0.36.0` (no `plugin-api` bump needed, `BaseTrack.artists`/`.album` already existed); clean-clone resolution re-verified (hit the previously-documented transient pub git-cache ref-lock error once, fixed the same way — clearing the stale cached clone under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Pub\Cache\git`). |
| 21 | Output devices | 🟡 Partial (closed 2026-08-14, was 0% for selection) — real, working **device listing** on every platform `audio_session` supports (speaker, wired headset, Bluetooth, USB, HDMI, via `AudioSession.getDevices()`/`devicesChangedEventStream`, no new native code — `audio_session` is already a `just_audio` dependency), plus real, working **device selection on Android 31+** (`AudioManager.setCommunicationDevice()`, exposed via `audio_session`'s `AndroidAudioManager`) reached through a new Settings → Playback & Audio → "Output devices" page (`lib/ui/settings/output_devices_page.dart`, `lib/core/output_device_controller.dart`). Below API 31, or off Android, selection degrades to an explicit "not supported here" message rather than silently failing — the list itself still works everywhere. Still missing: no USB DAC sample-rate/bit-depth control, no per-device volume/DSP profile beyond `EqualizerPlugin`'s existing per-*device-name* EQ keying (item 20). Not exercised against real hardware — same caveat as `BluetoothPlaybackPlugin`. Per-device volume closed 2026-08-16 — the "per-device volume" half of that same gap. New `Omnis-Plugins/lib/device_volume_plugin.dart` (`DeviceVolumePlugin`): mirrors `EqualizerPlugin`'s own per-device storage shape exactly (this plugin's `PluginStorage`, JSON-encoded, keyed by device name, subscribed to the same `IDeviceConnectivityProvider.deviceChanges` `BluetoothPlaybackPlugin` already registers) but deliberately diverges on one point — EQ bands safely reset to a flat 0.0 default for an unconfigured device, but volume has no safe "flat" equivalent, so a device with no saved profile is left alone entirely rather than forced to some fixed level, avoiding an unpleasant blast at full volume on first connect. `setVolumeForCurrentDevice(volume)` applies it immediately via `PluginContext.setVolume` (already a stable, pre-existing capability — no `packages/omnis_plugin_api` bump needed) and persists it under whichever device is currently connected (or a shared default slot with nothing connected); `forgetCurrentDeviceProfile()` removes a saved profile without itself changing the live volume. New settings-page UI (`plugin_settings` slot, the same pattern `EqualizerPlugin` uses) — a single slider labeled with the connected device's name, plus a "Forget saved volume" action, disabled with no profile saved. Registered in `bundled_plugins.dart` requiring sequential init after `BluetoothPlaybackPlugin`, the same documented ordering dependency `EqualizerPlugin` already has. 6 new tests in `test/device_volume_plugin_test.dart` (no-profile default state, apply-and-persist, 0..1 clamping, per-device switching leaving unconfigured devices untouched, persistence across a fresh plugin instance, forgetting a profile). All passed on the first real run. `Omnis-Plugins` suite: 512 passing (was 506), tagged `v0.34.0`, pushed. Cross-repo version bump: this repo's `pubspec.yaml` `omnis_plugins` ref bumped `v0.33.0` → `v0.34.0` (no `plugin-api` bump needed); clean-clone resolution re-verified. |
| 22 | Bit-perfect | 🟡 Partial for the informational half only — `BaseTrack` now carries real `codec`/`sampleRateHz`/`bitDepth`/`bitrateKbps`/`channels` fields (§18 — `plugin-api-v0.6.0`), populated by a new pure-Dart `AudioFormatReader` that parses real file headers (FLAC STREAMINFO, WAV fmt chunk, MP3 frame header incl. Xing/VBR average bitrate, and — closed 2026-08-14 — AIFF/AIFC's `COMM` chunk) — not guessed from the extension. AIFF's sample rate is stored as an 80-bit IEEE 754 extended-precision float (the one field none of the other three formats need, all using a plain 32-bit integer), decoded with its own from-scratch implementation; AIFC (the compressed variant) is distinguished from plain AIFF by its container's `FORM` type and gets a real, computed PCM bitrate only when its declared `compressionType` is actually uncompressed (`NONE`/`sowt`) — a genuinely compressed one (e.g. `ima4`) still reports real channels/sample-rate/bit-depth but leaves `bitrateKbps` `null` rather than reporting a number that would be wrong for it. Surfaced via a new "Audio info" dialog on each track (`library_page.dart`). Two real bugs found and fixed purely by writing hand-packed-byte tests (an independent `BigInt`-based 80-bit-float *encoder*, not just re-running the reader's own decode logic against itself): an off-by-two `COMM`-field offset (bit depth and sample rate were being read two bytes into the wrong fields, past where `numSampleFrames` actually ends) and a missing `.aifc` case in the format-dispatch `switch` (an AIFC file was silently falling through to the generic "unknown extension" branch instead of ever reaching the new AIFF/AIFC reader at all) — both caught only because the new tests asserted on hand-packed real bytes, not by inspecting the parsing code. 7 new tests in `test/audio_format_reader_test.dart`. Ogg Vorbis/Opus closed the same day too: real Ogg page parsing (`OggS` capture pattern, segment-table-summed packet length) reads the first page's identification-header packet for real sample rate and channel count, sniffed by payload signature (`"\x01vorbis"` vs `"OpusHead"`) rather than trusted from the `.ogg`/`.opus` extension, so either codec is correctly identified regardless of which extension it's saved under. Deliberately does **not** report a bitrate for either: Vorbis's header "nominal bitrate" field is an explicitly non-binding encoder hint, not a real number the way MP3's Xing-derived average or FLAC's file-size/duration-derived average are, and Opus's header carries no bitrate field at all — both left `null` rather than reported as if exact, the same stance AIFC's genuinely-compressed case already established. Opus always reports `48000`Hz regardless of the header's own "input sample rate" field, since that's what its decoder actually outputs (the field is informational provenance about the source material, not the playback rate). A well-formed Ogg page whose payload is neither Vorbis nor Opus (a real Ogg FLAC or Theora stream, for instance) gets a generic `'Ogg'` codec label rather than being misreported or crashing. 6 more tests. Still **0% on the DSP/output half**: no source→DSP→output *chain* display, no exclusive/WASAPI output mode. M4A closed 2026-08-14 too — the third of the four remaining extension-only formats, and the first with genuine box-tree nesting rather than a linear chunk/page format: `_readM4a` walks `moov`→`trak` (finding the real audio track via `mdia`/`hdlr`'s `soun` handler, tolerating a video track appearing first)→`mdia`→`minf`→`stbl`→`stsd`→the first sample entry, and `moov` itself is found by scanning top-level boxes from the start of the file rather than assumed to come first (some muxers put it after `mdat`). The sample entry's legacy QuickTime-compatibility fields (channel count/sample size/sample rate) are read first, then overridden with more authoritative values where available: AAC's real sample rate/channel count come from `esds`'s buried `AudioSpecificConfig` (a well-known real-world quirk — the legacy fields are commonly just placeholders for `mp4a` specifically, not a guess on this reader's part) via a full `ES_Descriptor`→`DecoderConfigDescriptor`→`DecoderSpecificInfo` variable-length-descriptor walk with an MSB-first bit reader for the non-byte-aligned config bits, and ALAC's nested `alac` magic-cookie box carries its own authoritative bit depth (a real, meaningful field for a *lossless* codec, unlike AAC), channels, sample rate, and average bitrate directly in a fixed 24-byte layout — no bit-level parsing needed there at all. AAC never reports a `bitDepth`, matching this reader's existing stance for Ogg Vorbis/Opus: it's lossy, decoded at whatever precision the decoder chooses, not a real fixed-width PCM value. `esds`'s real average-bitrate field is used when present and nonzero; a duration-derived fallback (`mdhd`'s timescale/duration against the whole file size) covers the rest, the same technique FLAC's reader already uses. A missing/malformed `esds` or `alac` cookie falls back to the legacy fields rather than losing everything already parsed — verified with a dedicated test using a real `mp4a` entry with no `esds` at all. 6 new hand-packed-byte tests in `test/audio_format_reader_test.dart` (independent `mp4Box`/`buildEsds`/`buildAlacCookie`/`buildM4aFile` encoders, not the reader's own logic run backwards), all passing on the first real run — zero bugs found this time, the third format in a row (after AIFF's two and Ogg's zero) where the established from-scratch-encoder-test methodology paid off before the code shipped rather than after. One of the six specifically proves the video-track-skipping logic (a video `trak` placed before the audio one), and another proves `moov`-after-`mdat` ordering is handled, not just assumed canonical. A bare `.aac` file (an ADTS elementary stream, not an MP4 container — a different frame-sync format entirely, closer in shape to MP3) is deliberately still label-only; that's real, separate work, not a gap in this increment's scope. Full suite: 739 passing (was 733), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. WMA closed 2026-08-15, the fourth and last remaining extension-only format: `_readWma` parses ASF's flat GUID-object header (Header Object → child objects, no nesting the way MP4 boxes have), reading the audio Stream Properties Object's embedded `WAVEFORMATEX` structure — the same structure WAV's own `fmt` chunk carries, just one level deeper here — for real sample rate/channels/bit depth, plus a real encoder-declared average bitrate (`nAvgBytesPerSec`) preferred over the File Properties Object's Maximum Bitrate (a ceiling, not an average), itself preferred over a duration-derived fallback. Correctly finds the *audio* Stream Properties Object by its Stream Type GUID even when a video one appears first in the same file. A handful of well-known format tags (WMA/WMA Pro/WMA Lossless/PCM) get a specific label; an unrecognized one still reports real numeric fields with a generic `WMA (0x....)` label rather than guessing which codec it is. 6 new hand-packed-byte tests in `test/audio_format_reader_test.dart`, all passing on the first real run — the third format this week (after Ogg and M4A) with zero bugs found, against AIFF's two. Full suite: 748 passing (was 742), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. **Format detection is now real for every container this reader recognizes** except a bare `.aac` file (an ADTS elementary stream, not a container at all — deliberately out of scope, a different frame-sync format closer in shape to MP3). Bare `.aac`/ADTS closed 2026-08-16 — the one remaining recognized extension left label-only, confirmed by reading `audio_format_reader.dart` directly (`case 'aac': return const AudioFormatInfo(codec: 'AAC');`, no parsing at all). New `AudioFormatReader._readAdts` scans for the 12-bit `0xFFF` sync word (the same frame-sync-search shape `_readMp3` already uses) and decodes the fixed 7-byte ADTS header's sampling-frequency-index/channel-configuration fields — reusing `_aacSampleRates`, the identical MPEG-4 table `_parseEsds` already reads out of a raw `AudioSpecificConfig`, rather than a second copy of it. Unlike every other format here, ADTS has no file-wide duration/bitrate field and no `Xing`-style tag convention, so average bitrate is computed honestly over a *sampled window* (the same 64KB window `_readMp3`'s own scan already reads): every consecutive frame found there is summed by its real frame_length and real sample count (`number_of_raw_data_blocks × 1024`) into a genuine local average — documented explicitly as windowed, not whole-file, the same "don't guess what can't be derived honestly" stance Ogg Vorbis/Opus already take for their own unreportable bitrate fields. A reserved/invalid frequency index, or a frame this window doesn't fully contain, is excluded rather than corrupting the result; a file with no valid sync anywhere degrades to the pre-existing bare `AudioFormatInfo(codec: 'AAC')` fallback. Reports `AAC (ADTS)` normally, `AAC (ADTS, CRC)` when the header's own `protection_absent` bit says a CRC follows. **Real regression caught and fixed before it shipped**: `library_statistics.dart` and `library_cleanup_analyzer.dart` each keep their own duplicated lossy-codec-label set containing the exact string `'AAC'` for lossless/lossy classification and low-quality-file flagging — since a successfully-parsed bare-ADTS file now reports `'AAC (ADTS)'`/`'AAC (ADTS, CRC)'` instead of always `'AAC'`, those two sets would have silently stopped classifying real ADTS files as lossy; both sets now include the new labels. Also added `'aac'` to `library_cleanup_analyzer.dart`'s `_fullyParsedExtensions` (used by the "corrupt files" heuristic) now that it's genuinely parsed — updated one existing test whose name/assertion ("a bare .aac file (deliberately unparsed) is never flagged as corrupt") was now factually wrong given the new parsing, rather than leaving a stale claim in the suite. 10 new tests in `test/audio_format_reader_test.dart` (real sample rate/channels, mono, 8-channel/7.1 mapping, CRC-present label, windowed average bitrate with a hand-computed expected value, `number_of_raw_data_blocks > 1` contributing extra samples, a truncated trailing frame correctly excluded rather than mis-counted, a reserved frequency index falling back cleanly, no-sync-found and empty-file fallbacks) plus 1 new test in `test/library_cleanup_analyzer_test.dart` (a successfully-parsed ADTS track is never flagged corrupt). All new/hand-packed-byte tests passed on the first real run — the fourth format parsed this pass (after Ogg, M4A, WMA) with zero *decoding* bugs found; the one real bug was the cross-file lossy-set regression above, caught by deliberately checking every exact-string `'AAC'` usage before considering this done, not by a failing test. Full suite: 1316 passing (was 1305), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. Item 22 now has no further named gaps — format detection is real for every container/elementary-stream extension this reader recognizes. |
