| 23 | Audio analysis | 🟡 Partial — `AudioAnalysisPlugin` gets real audio-derived BPM/key/mood/genre from a self-hosted Essentia service (not bundled, user must deploy it); no loudness/dynamic-range measurement, no on-device/offline analysis. |

## Phase 4 — Plugin platform

> **Audited 2026-08-13.** Full findings in [OMNIS_2_0_MISSED_DEEP_PHASE.md](OMNIS_2_0_MISSED_DEEP_PHASE.md#phase-4--plugin-platform). This phase is the most under-credited by the old blanket tracker — most of it is real.

| # | Item | Status |
| --- | ------ | -------- |
| 24 | Capability interfaces | ✅ Solid — a real typed `ServiceRegistry` (`register`/`get<T>`/`getAll<T>`, multi-provider support) with 9 interfaces (`ILyricsProvider`, `IMetadataProvider`, `IQueueBuilder`, etc.), used throughout. External (downloaded/sandboxed) plugins can now register as providers for 4 of the 9 — `lyrics`, `queue_builder`, and, added 2026-08-13, `play_history` and `artist_image` (`SandboxedPlayHistoryProvider`/`SandboxedArtistImageProvider` in `plugin_sandbox_services.dart`, wired through `PluginManager._registerProvidedServices`). The remaining 5 stay deliberately unbridged: `IMetadataProvider`/`IFileTagWriter`/`IAudioAnalysisProvider` write results back into real library storage or real audio files (a materially bigger trust decision, left for its own review), and `IVisualizerProvider` needs a genuine live `Stream` that the synchronous `callHook` request/response shape can't cleanly bridge without new polling-`Timer` + disposal machinery. |
| 25 | Plugin lifecycle | ✅ Solid mechanics — real sandboxing (`PluginSandbox.run`, catches + 8s-timeouts every hook, logs health, never propagates), a real interpreted sandbox (`dart_eval`) for downloaded plugins. Gap: no formal state-machine enum (two booleans instead), and `dart_eval` runs on the same thread (documented in `docs/PLUGIN_SECURITY.md`) so a non-yielding downloaded-plugin loop can still hang the UI. |
| 26 | Dependency resolution | 🟡 Partial — bundled-plugin ordering (`requiresSequentialInit` + a two-round init) unchanged. For *external* (downloaded) plugins, closed 2026-08-14: `PluginManifest` now parses a `dependencies:` list of other plugin ids, enforced at install time (`PluginManager._registerInstalledPlugin` throws a `PluginInstallException` naming exactly which declared dependencies aren't currently installed, blocking the install rather than letting a plugin register into a broken state) and re-checkable on demand via new `PluginManager.missingDependenciesFor(plugin)` — the Plugins page shows a warning row on any installed plugin whose dependency has since been uninstalled, closing the "no detection of a dependency disappearing" half of the gap too, not just a one-time install-time gate. `minOmnisVersion` enforcement closed 2026-08-14 too: new `lib/core/omnis_version.dart` (`omnisCoreVersion`, a manually-maintained constant kept in sync with `pubspec.yaml`'s own `version:` field — reading it live would need a new dependency, `package_info_plus`, judged disproportionate to this one narrow check) compared against a manifest's declared `min_omnis_version:` via the existing `compareVersions` (`semver.dart`, already used for update-checking) in the same `_registerInstalledPlugin` gate the dependency check uses — a plugin requiring a newer Omnis than is running is refused at install/update time with a message naming both versions, rather than installing into a plugin that may not actually work. 4 new tests in `test/plugin_system_test.dart` (blocks a too-new requirement, allows an already-met one, allows an exact match — "at least," not "strictly newer than" — and confirms no `min_omnis_version:` never gates at all). A missing dependency is still detected/surfaced, not auto-resolved (no "install this dependency for me" flow) — the one gap this item's own remaining text named that's still genuinely open. The "install this dependency for me" flow closed 2026-08-16 — confirmed still open by reading `plugins_page.dart`'s missing-dependency warning row directly: an icon + text naming the missing id(s), no action attached. New pure `findCatalogEntryForPluginId(id, catalog)` in `lib/core/plugin_catalog.dart` — an exact-match lookup against `CatalogPluginEntry.folder` (the same string every bundled/published plugin's own id already is), returning `null` when the missing dependency isn't in the discoverable catalog rather than offering a button that couldn't actually work. Wired into `plugins_page.dart`'s existing missing-dependency warning row: each missing id with a catalog match gets a real "Install `<id>`" button reusing `_installFromCatalog` — the exact same one-tap install path the catalog cards already use, so no second install implementation; a missing id with no catalog match keeps today's warning-only text, unchanged. **Two real test-writing bugs caught and fixed, not production bugs**: (1) installing a plugin whose declared dependency isn't installed *yet* is itself rejected by `_registerInstalledPlugin`'s existing gate, so producing a genuine "missing dependency" state for the widget test needs installing both then uninstalling the dependency — the same two-step `plugin_system_test.dart`'s own item-26 tests already use, not a single install alone; (2) the two new widget tests initially hung for the full 10-minute test timeout (`dart:isolate _RawReceivePort._handleMessage` in the stack) because `PluginManager.installFromPath`'s real `dart_eval` plugin-loading ran *outside* `tester.runAsync()` — every other install-driving test in this file already wraps that work inside `runAsync()` (`_RoutingClient`/`MockClient`-backed tests), fixed by moving the install calls inside the same block. A third, smaller test-writing mistake fixed alongside those: a negative assertion checking for no "Install" button used a bare `find.textContaining('Install ')`, which also matched this page's own pre-existing "Install a plugin" section header — narrowed to the specific button text. New `test/plugin_catalog_test.dart` (5 tests: match found, no match, empty catalog, case-sensitivity, the real `officialPluginCatalog` fallback matching its own `sample_logger` entry) plus a new "Missing dependency install (item 26)" group in `test/plugins_page_test.dart` (2 widget tests: a catalog-known missing dependency gets a real Install button; an unknown one stays warning-only). All new tests passed after the three fixes above. Full suite: 1323 passing (was 1316), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. Item 26 now has no further named gaps in this tracker. |
| 27 | Permissions | ✅ Solid — declared manifest permissions map to real `dart_eval` grants, shown in plain English before any plugin code runs. Granular network scoping closed 2026-08-15: `network:host.example.com` now grants a real, enforced `dart_eval` `NetworkPermission.url(...)` for that exact host, checked against the actual requested URL at call time by a new `httpGet` bridge function — the first real network capability bridged into the sandbox at all (`network` was previously declarative-only, populating just the install-confirmation dialog's text with no bridged function that could reach the network regardless of what a manifest declared). A bare `network` (no host) still grants unrestricted access via `NetworkPermission.any`, for manifests written before scoping existed. `storage` remains the one still-coarse, all-or-nothing category (`FilesystemPermission.any`, unchanged) — no per-directory scoping exists at the `dart_eval` layer for that one to build on the way `NetworkPermission` existed ready-made for network. |
| 28 | Plugin health | 🟡 Partial — real, reactive health records (created whenever a sandboxed call throws/times out) with a live UI section on the Plugins page. Gained a real per-plugin retry/reset action (closed 2026-08-14): `PluginManager.resetPlugin()` runs a genuine `disable()`→`enable()` cycle (not a full re-`initialize()`) and clears that plugin's own health records afterward via new `PluginSandbox.clearHealthFor(pluginId)`, reached via a "Reset" button on each health record's card. Looks the plugin up by id (a health record only ever carries id/name, not a live reference) and fails soft with a clear message if the plugin's since been uninstalled, rather than crashing. Auto-disable on repeated failure closed the same day: `PluginManager` now subscribes to `PluginSandbox`'s own health-change notifications (`_checkAutoDisable`, wired in the constructor, unsubscribed in `dispose()`) and, whenever a plugin racks up 5 failures within a rolling 5-minute window, calls the same `disablePlugin()` a user tapping "Disable" in the Plugins page would — real `disable()` hook invocation, `AppSettings` persistence, service unregistration, not a special-cased shortcut. Deliberately counts by *time window* over real failure timestamps rather than a true consecutive-since-last-success counter the way `PlaybackWatchdog`'s `_consecutiveFailures` does — a genuine data-availability difference, not an inconsistency: `PluginHealthRecord`s only ever exist for failures, there's no corresponding "this hook call succeeded" event recorded anywhere a true consecutive counter could reset against. Checking `plugin.enabled` before acting means a plugin already disabled (including by this exact mechanism moments earlier) is never redundantly re-disabled, even if its own `disable()` hook itself throws and adds one more health record — `disablePlugin()` sets `enabled = false` before invoking that hook. 5 new tests in `test/plugin_architecture_test.dart`, all passing on the first run: crosses the threshold and disables, stays enabled one failure short of it, failures for one plugin never affect an unrelated healthy one, the real `disable()` hook fires and the choice persists to `AppSettings` (not a shortcut), and a plugin already tripped doesn't get redundantly re-disabled by further failures. Full suite: 696 passing (was 691), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. Still no heartbeat (a silently-hung, non-throwing plugin still isn't detected) and no dedicated health-center page (still a section within the Plugins page). The dedicated health-center page closed 2026-08-16 — confirmed still a `plugins_page.dart` inline section by reading it directly (one `Card` per raw `PluginHealthRecord`, not per plugin). New pure `lib/core/plugin_health_summary.dart`: `summarizeHealth(records, {now, window, criticalThreshold})` groups records by plugin id into one `PluginHealthSummary` each (total + recent-window failure counts, most-recent reason, a `degraded`/`critical` severity tier), sorted worst-first — critical before degraded, then more recent-window failures, then most-recent timestamp. `window`/`criticalThreshold` default to the exact values `PluginManager`'s own auto-disable check already uses (5 failures/5 minutes) — duplicated rather than cross-imported, the same "small stable value, not worth a cross-file coupling" reasoning `library_cleanup_analyzer.dart`'s own duplicated codec/extension sets already establish — so a summary's "critical" tier means exactly what the auto-disable mechanism itself would act on, not a second, differently-tuned notion of "bad." No `PluginSandbox`/`PluginManager` dependency, fully unit-testable with a plain record list. New `lib/ui/plugin_health_page.dart` (`PluginHealthPage`) — one card per plugin (not per failure), an expandable "View details" for the raw per-failure records, Reset (the same `PluginManager.resetPlugin` lookup-by-id-and-reset shape the old inline section already used), and "Dismiss all". `plugins_page.dart`'s own health section shrank to a summary card ("N plugin(s) with failures") linking into the new page — the same "link out rather than keep growing inline" shape `library_page.dart` already uses for its statistics/cleanup-report pages — plus a new "Plugin health" Settings-search entry. 8 new tests in `test/plugin_health_summary_test.dart` (empty input, per-plugin grouping, most-recent reason/timestamp, recent-vs-total count distinction, severity boundary at the threshold, critical-before-degraded sort, same-tier recent-count tiebreak, cross-plugin isolation) and 6 widget tests in `test/plugin_health_page_test.dart` (empty state, a real sandboxed failure's Reset flow — moved here from `plugins_page_test.dart` verbatim, since that's this functionality's real home now — the already-uninstalled-plugin edge case likewise moved, expand/collapse, Dismiss all, multiple failing plugins each getting their own card); `plugins_page_test.dart` gained 2 replacement tests for its own shrunk summary card (healthy-with-no-link state, and tapping the summary genuinely opening `PluginHealthPage`). **One real test-infrastructure bug caught and fixed, not a production bug**: the new `plugin_health_page_test.dart`'s tests that register a real plugin and call `PluginManager.initializeAll()` initially hung for the full 10-minute test timeout — `PluginStorage.initialize()` (called from `initPlugin`) calls the real `SharedPreferences.getInstance()`, a platform-channel call with nothing to answer it in a test environment lacking the standard `SharedPreferences.setMockInitialValues({})` mock every other stateful test file in this repo already sets up; the brand-new file simply hadn't added its own `setUp()` for it yet. Fixed by adding it — all 6 tests then passed instantly on the very next run. All other new/moved tests passed on the first real run. Full suite: 1337 passing (was 1323), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. Item 28's one remaining named gap is a heartbeat for a silently-hung, non-throwing plugin — a materially different, larger detection mechanism, not a same-size follow-on. |
| 29 | Plugin updates | 🟡 Partial (was genuine 0%, closed 2026-08-13) — real update detection: `PluginInstaller.fetchRemoteManifest` fetches just the published `omnis_plugin.yaml` via `raw.githubusercontent.com` (not a full zip download), `PluginManager.checkForUpdates()` compares it against every installed external plugin's version with a real numeric (not lexical) semver-style comparison, `updatePlugin()` re-downloads and replaces it while preserving the user's enabled/disabled choice. Wired into a "Check for updates" button + per-plugin "Update available" banner on the Plugins page. Backup-before-update + rollback closed 2026-08-14: `PluginInstaller.installFromUrl` resolves a plugin's install directory *deterministically* from its source URL, so re-installing the same plugin during an update targets the exact same directory and unconditionally wipes it before extracting — previously, a download that failed partway (network drop, corrupt zip, invalid manifest) left the plugin's files gone or partially written while its `ManagedPlugin` record stayed registered with its services already torn down: installed in name only, silently broken, with no way back. `updatePlugin()` now snapshots the current directory (`PluginInstaller.backupPluginDirectory`, copied to a location *outside* the plugins root so `listInstalled()`'s directory scan never mistakes a backup for a second copy of the same plugin) before attempting the download; on success the backup is discarded, on failure it's restored and the plugin re-registered from it before the original error is rethrown — a failed update now leaves the previous working version running exactly as before, not a corpse. A rollback that itself fails (extremely unlikely, but not assumed impossible) surfaces *both* failures in one message rather than silently swallowing the second. 9 new tests: 6 direct unit tests for `backupPluginDirectory`/`restorePluginBackup`/`discardPluginBackup` in `test/plugin_installer_test.dart` (including a nested-subdirectory case, proving it's a real recursive copy, not a shallow one), 3 in `test/plugin_updates_test.dart` against a new client double that can fail the zip download specifically after a real install already succeeded — verifying the rollback happens, the error message names the original failure, and a plugin that was rolled back can still be updated successfully afterward (not left in some half-updated limbo). All passed on the first real run. Full suite: 691 passing (was 682), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. Still gaps: no automatic/background checking (user-initiated only), and update detection only works for a GitHub `tree`/bare-repo source URL — a plugin installed from a direct `.zip` link can't be checked this way. Automatic/background checking closed 2026-08-16 — this entry's own text already named it as the remaining gap. Confirmed genuinely unbuilt by checking that `checkForUpdates` was called from exactly one place (`plugins_page.dart`'s manual button handler) and grepping for `autoUpdateCheck`/`lastKnownUpdates`/`PluginUpdateScheduler` — zero hits before this change. Mirrors item 4/50's `BackupScheduler`/`BackupService.maybeRunAutomaticBackup` template closely: three new `AppSettings` fields (`autoUpdateCheckEnabled`, default `false` — the same opt-in stance unattended background work always gets here; `autoUpdateCheckIntervalDays`, default 3 — more frequent than the weekly backup default, since a manifest-only check is a cheap read-only network call, not a disk write; `lastPluginUpdateCheckAt`), a new pure `lib/core/plugin_update_scheduler.dart` (`PluginUpdateScheduler.isDue`, structurally identical to `BackupScheduler.isDue` but kept as its own independent class rather than sharing it — this codebase's established convention is one small scheduler per concern), and `PluginManager.maybeCheckForUpdatesAutomatically` (a thin wrapper: no-ops when disabled/not due, otherwise calls the pre-existing `checkForUpdates()`, caches the result into a new `lastKnownUpdates` field, and stamps the timestamp — even when the check finds nothing, since "checked, found nothing" is still a completed check). Wired into `MainCore.initialize()` as an unawaited fire-and-forget call right alongside the existing backup one. `plugins_page.dart` now primes `_availableUpdates` from `pluginManager.lastKnownUpdates` in `initState` (so a background check that already ran before the page opened shows its result immediately rather than staying blank until the button is tapped) and gained a new "Automatic update checks" `SwitchListTile` next to the existing manual button. 5 new tests in `test/plugin_update_scheduler_test.dart` (mirroring `backup_scheduler_test.dart`'s own boundary cases), a new group in `test/plugin_updates_test.dart` (disabled no-op, first-run-always-due, not-yet-due no-op, due-after-interval finds and caches updates, timestamp stamped even on a nothing-found check), and 2 widget tests in `test/plugins_page_test.dart` (the toggle renders/defaults off/persists; a cached background-check result shows the banner without tapping "Check for updates") — two real test-writing bugs caught and fixed before shipping, not production bugs: both new widget tests initially tapped/asserted on content below the default 800×600 test viewport's fold without an `ensureVisible` first, the same sliver-cache-extent-adjacent issue this session has hit and fixed many times before. A third, genuinely separate real regression was only caught by running the *full* suite, not this feature's own new/touched test files in isolation: the new "Automatic update checks" toggle's two-line subtitle added just enough height above `plugins_page.dart`'s installed-plugins list to push it entirely past the default viewport's sliver mount/cache-extent boundary — not just off-screen but genuinely unbuilt — which broke a *pre-existing, unrelated* test in `test/plugin_settings_page_test.dart` ("tapping a plugin in the Plugins list opens its settings page") that had been using `ensureVisible` (which needs a widget to already exist in the tree) rather than `dragUntilVisible`. Root-caused via a throwaway debug harness dumping every `Text` widget actually present at the default viewport vs. a deliberately oversized one, confirming the plugin row was never built at all rather than a real logic bug; fixed in the test file by switching to `dragUntilVisible`, the same fix this exact bug class has needed several times before elsewhere in this session. Full suite: 1242 passing (was 1230), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. Item 29's one remaining named gap is update detection for a direct `.zip`-link source URL (currently only GitHub `tree`/bare-repo URLs are checkable). |
| 30 | Marketplace/catalog | ✅ Solid — a real permission-confirmation install flow exists. Closed the "not a fetched `catalog.json`" half of this item's gap (2026-08-14): new `PluginInstaller.fetchCatalog()` fetches a real, live `catalog.json` published at the root of the `Omnis-Plugins` repo (`raw.githubusercontent.com`, same lightweight single-file approach update-checking already used — no GitHub API, no auth, no rate limit to manage), replacing the fully hardcoded list this app previously shipped with no live source at all. Falls back to that same list (now `officialPluginCatalog` in `lib/core/plugin_catalog.dart`, moved out of the UI layer) when the fetch fails — offline, GitHub unreachable, a malformed response — so the catalog card is never left empty. Verified the published file is genuinely live at the exact URL the app fetches, not just against a mocked test. Closed the "not browsable/searchable" half the same day: `PluginsPage` gained a `TextField` above the catalog list filtering `_catalog` by name or description, case-insensitively, via a new `_filteredCatalog` getter (`library_search.dart`'s own "blank query means no filtering" convention, not a bespoke rule) — with an explicit "No plugins match "…"." message rather than a silent empty gap when nothing matches. Found via a real test run, not review: adding the search box's height pushed the "Installed plugins" section's "Check for updates" button and (in a separate test file) a plugin's settings tap target below the default test viewport's sliver mount/cache-extent boundary, breaking 4 previously-passing tests across `plugins_page_test.dart` and `plugin_settings_page_test.dart` — fixed with `tester.ensureVisible(...)` before each affected tap, the same fix this bug class has needed several times elsewhere this session. 5 new tests in `test/plugins_page_test.dart` (`Plugin catalog search (item 30)` group): empty query shows everything, name match, description match, no-match message, clearing restores the full list. Full suite: 733 passing (was 728), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. Still no plugin registry/curation review of what's *in* the catalog beyond what a maintainer manually publishes (`docs/PLUGIN_SECURITY.md`'s "No plugin registry or curation today" still holds for the *trust* question — this item's remaining gaps are all on that trust side now, not discovery/browsing). |

## Phase 5 — Connectivity

> **Audited 2026-08-13.** Full findings in [OMNIS_2_0_MISSED_DEEP_PHASE.md](OMNIS_2_0_MISSED_DEEP_PHASE.md#phase-5--connectivity).

| # | Item | Status |
| --- | ------ | -------- |
| 31 | OpenSubsonic | 🟢 Solid but unverified (was genuine 0%, closed 2026-08-13) — new `OpenSubsonicPlugin` (`Omnis-Plugins`) is a real client to the OpenSubsonic/Subsonic REST API: token-based auth (`t = md5(password+salt)`, never sends the plaintext password as a query param), `ping.view` connection testing, `search3.view` search returning genuinely playable `BaseTrack`s (`type: TrackType.subsonic`, real `streamUrl` pointing at the server's own `stream.view` endpoint — plays through the existing engine with zero special-casing, unlike Spotify's DRM-blocked metadata-only imports). Settings UI (server/username/password + "Test connection" + inline search-and-play) reached via the Plugins page's own settings slot, the same pattern Spotify/YouTube import use. 17 tests against a mocked HTTP client, including one that independently recomputes the MD5 token from the salt actually sent to prove the auth math is right. **Not exercised against a real Navidrome/Subsonic/Airsonic server** — protocol-level correctness only, the same caveat already applied to Spotify/YouTube. |
| 32 | Navidrome | 🟢 Solid but unverified — Navidrome implements the OpenSubsonic API, so item 31's `OpenSubsonicPlugin` already works against it in principle; no Navidrome-specific code exists or is needed. Same unverified-against-a-real-server caveat as item 31. |
| 33 | Jellyfin | 🟢 Solid but unverified (was genuine 0%, closed 2026-08-13) — new `JellyfinPlugin` (`Omnis-Plugins`) is a real client to Jellyfin's REST API: session-token auth via `/Users/AuthenticateByName` (kept in memory only, transparently re-authenticated once on a `401`), `/Items` search returning genuinely playable `BaseTrack`s (new `TrackType.jellyfin` on `plugin-api-v0.9.0`, a real `streamUrl` at Jellyfin's own `/Audio/{id}/stream` endpoint — plays with zero `AudioEngine` special-casing). Same settings-slot UI pattern as `OpenSubsonicPlugin`/Spotify/YouTube import. 17 new tests against a mocked HTTP client, including one proving the 401-retry logic is bounded to exactly one retry rather than looping forever against a server that keeps rejecting. **Not exercised against a real Jellyfin server** — protocol-level correctness only, same caveat as items 31/32/36/37. |
| 34 | Plex | 🟢 Solid but unverified (was genuine 0%, closed 2026-08-13) — new `PlexPlugin` (`Omnis-Plugins`) is a real client to Plex Media Server's REST API: `X-Plex-Token` header auth (the token itself pasted in by the user, not obtained via the full `plex.tv` sign-in/PIN flow — a deliberate, documented scope cut, the standard entry point every third-party Plex client starts from), `/search` returning genuinely playable `BaseTrack`s (new `TrackType.plex` on `plugin-api-v0.10.0`, real `streamUrl` built from the nested `Media[0].Part[0].key` — plays with zero `AudioEngine` special-casing). Plex's `/search` mixes every media type in one response; only `"type": "track"` entries are kept. 18 new tests against a mocked HTTP client. **Not exercised against a real Plex server** — protocol-level correctness only, same caveat as items 31/32/33/36/37. |
| 35 | DLNA/UPnP | 🟡 Partial (was genuine 0%, closed 2026-08-13) — new `DlnaPlugin` (`Omnis-Plugins`) is a real SSDP+SOAP/DIDL-Lite client: UDP multicast `M-SEARCH` discovery (behind an injected `SsdpTransport` so the protocol logic is fully unit-tested without a real socket), device-description XML parsing (`friendlyName`, `ContentDirectory` service `controlURL`, honoring an explicit `URLBase` when present), a real `ContentDirectory#Browse` SOAP call, and DIDL-Lite parsing into folders (containers) + genuinely playable `BaseTrack`s (new `TrackType.dlna` on `plugin-api-v0.12.0`, `streamUrl` = the real `<res>` URL, no auth needed on a trusted local network — unlike every other server type this session added). Folder-navigation UI (discover → open a server → browse into folders → play a track), not a search box — DLNA's optional `Search` action isn't consistently implemented across servers, `Browse` is the only universally-supported one. 16 new tests, including one that caught and fixed a real bug during development: `friendlyName`/`URLBase` live under `<root><device>`, not as direct children of `<root>` — a naive direct-children-only lookup silently fell back to the server's IP instead of its real name. **A real, documented, unverified-in-this-environment limitation, not just "unverified against a live server"**: Android filters incoming WiFi multicast by default, and receiving SSDP responses normally needs an acquired `WifiManager.MulticastLock`, which this plugin does not implement (would need a platform channel) — discovery may find nothing on some real Android devices even with a real, reachable DLNA server present, despite the protocol logic itself being correct and fully tested. |
| 36 | Spotify | 🟢 Solid, unverified — real PKCE OAuth (`SpotifyAuth`), playlist import (`SpotifyImportPlugin`), and Spotify-Connect remote control (`SpotifyPlaybackPlugin`), all deliberately metadata/remote-only (DRM prevents local decode — by design, not a shortfall). Self-flagged ⚠️ in `Omnis-Plugins/README.md`: never exercised against a real account. |
| 37 | YouTube | 🟢 Solid, unverified — same shape as Spotify: OAuth (`YoutubeAuth`), playlist import + public search (`YoutubeMusicImportPlugin`), and playback via YouTube's own embedded IFrame player (`YoutubePlaybackPlugin`), deliberately not stream-extraction (ToS). Self-flagged ⚠️: never run on a real device/OAuth client. |
| 38 | Other providers | 🟡 Partial (closed 2026-08-14 for Emby, was genuine 0%) — new `EmbyPlugin` (`Omnis-Plugins`) closes the one provider in this item's list with an open, self-hostable, keyless-registration server: real REST client to a self-hosted Emby server (`TrackType.emby` on `plugin-api-v0.13.0`), directly playable `streamUrl`s, same settings-slot UI pattern as OpenSubsonic/Jellyfin/Plex. Deliberately not implemented as a `JellyfinPlugin` subclass/shared-client despite the two protocols being close cousins (Jellyfin forked from Emby in 2018 and still shares its `X-Emby-Authorization` header name) — kept as its own plugin since they're two different servers a user could each run, same reasoning already applied to Plex/DLNA. Still genuine 0% for Apple Music/SoundCloud/Bandcamp/Tidal/Qobuz — each requires a closed or paid developer registration this environment can't obtain, judged not tractable. `ArtistImagePlugin`'s Deezer call remains artist-photo lookup only, not a Deezer music provider. **Not exercised against a real Emby server** — protocol-level correctness only, same caveat as every other server-connectivity plugin this session. |

## Phase 6 — Discovery

> **Audited 2026-08-13.** Full findings in [OMNIS_2_0_MISSED_DEEP_PHASE.md](OMNIS_2_0_MISSED_DEEP_PHASE.md#phase-6--discovery).

| # | Item | Status |
| --- | ------ | -------- |
| 39 | Recommendations | 🟡 Partial — the "Moods" page (`_MoodsPage`) tries each registered `IQueueBuilder` in turn. Gained a second, genuinely data-driven algorithm this session (closed 2026-08-14): "Forgotten Favorites" (`QueuePresetPlugin`, alongside its existing BPM/genre presets) — real most-played tracks (via `IPlayHistoryProvider`, backed by `ScrobblePlugin`'s persisted play history) that have dropped out of the recent-plays window, one of the spec's own named algorithms. The first real consumer of play-history data for a recommendation, closing part of "favorites/ratings/history data exists but nothing consumes it." Deliberately returns empty (not the sibling presets' whole-library-shuffle fallback) when there's no real history yet — an honest "nothing to recommend," not a misleading substitute. A third algorithm, "Rediscover," joined the list the same day: tracks rated 4+ stars (via the `IRatingsProvider` interface `RatingsPlugin` now implements, added for item 42's `rating:` rule condition) that haven't shown up in recent plays — the first recommendation to consume `RatingsPlugin`'s explicit-preference data rather than `ScrobblePlugin`'s implicit play-count data, and a genuinely different claim from "Forgotten Favorites": a track can be rated five stars after one listen and never played much since, never ranking among the *most played* (so "Forgotten Favorites" would never surface it), while "Rediscover" catches it precisely because it looks at explicit rating instead of play frequency. Requires **both** `IRatingsProvider` and `IPlayHistoryProvider` — missing either degrades to empty rather than silently dropping half the claim (without history there's no way to tell a track just played from one genuinely unheard for a while, and returning every highly-rated track regardless would mislabel something just played as a "rediscovery"). "Rediscover" is itself one of the spec's own named algorithms (§39), not an invented substitute. 10 new tests in `test/queue_preset_plugin_test.dart`, all passing on the first run, mirroring "Forgotten Favorites"' existing fake-provider test harness. `Omnis-Plugins` suite: 235 passing, tagged `v0.18.0`. Cross-repo version bump: this repo's `pubspec.yaml` `omnis_plugins` ref bumped from `v0.17.0` to `v0.18.0`, clean-clone resolution re-verified. Main-repo full suite: 682 passing, `flutter analyze` clean in both repos. A fourth, "Similar Track," closed 2026-08-15: new `lib/core/track_similarity.dart` (`similarityScore`/`findSimilarTracks`) computes real weighted distance over `BaseTrack`'s own genre/mood/bpm/key fields (Jaccard over the genre set, exact-match mood/key, linear BPM falloff), only ever comparing signals present on **both** tracks (renormalized against just those present, so a track missing one signal isn't unfairly penalized) — the first genuine "sounds like this" computation in either repo (closing part of item 40 too). A seed track with no comparable data at all, or a candidate sharing zero signal with it, is excluded rather than given an arbitrary/misleading score, the same "don't manufacture a claim the data can't support" stance "Forgotten Favorites"/"Rediscover" already established. Wired into `library_page.dart` as a new "Play similar" per-track menu action, reusing the exact `setQueue`+`playAt` pattern every other queue action there already uses. A fifth, "Favorites Mix," closed 2026-08-15: new `IFavoritesProvider` capability interface (`packages/omnis_plugin_api`, mirroring `IRatingsProvider`'s exact shape) that `FavoritesPlugin` now implements and registers, closing this same entry's own "`FavoritesPlugin` data still isn't used by any recommendation" line — `QueuePresetPlugin` gained a third data-driven preset, a shuffled sample of the listener's favorited tracks resolved against the current library, with the same "resolve by id, drop what's missing" and "empty rather than a misleading whole-library fallback" contracts every sibling data-driven preset here already has. Still missing every other named algorithm (Similar Artist, Daily/Weekly Mix, Discovery, Deep Cuts, New Releases, Energy Flow); no provider-neutral recommendation framework beyond the existing `IQueueBuilder` fan-out; `library_page.dart` itself has no widget-test harness at all (a pre-existing gap, not introduced here), so "Play similar" is `flutter analyze`-verified only, same as every other action on that page's menu — the underlying `similarityScore`/`findSimilarTracks` logic itself has full unit coverage (29 tests, `test/track_similarity_test.dart`). "Forgotten Music" (spec §37) closed 2026-08-15 as a genuinely separate gap from "Forgotten Favorites" above, not a duplicate: confirmed by reading `Omnis-Plugins/lib/queue_preset_plugin.dart`'s `_buildForgottenFavorites` — it only ever considers a listener's top-N *most-played* tracks (`IPlayHistoryProvider.mostPlayedIds`) and returns a queue directly with no browsable list at all, so a track played only a handful of times and then abandoned, or never played at all, could never surface through it; §37's own "147 tracks you haven't heard in 6+ months" browsing UI was still genuinely unbuilt (grepped `lib/` for `Forgotten`/`forgotten`, only hit `track_similarity.dart`'s unrelated "sounds like" doc comment and the Moods grid's existing preset tile). New pure `lib/core/forgotten_tracks.dart` (`findForgottenTracks(tracks, lastPlayedById, {threshold, now})`) covers every owned track: absent from the map at all (never played) sorts first, then stale entries oldest-last-played-first, both are "forgotten," a track played more recently than `now - threshold` is not. New `PlayHistoryStore.lastPlayedByTrackId()` exposes the raw, uncapped, unsorted `trackId -> lastPlayedAt` map the query needs — every existing accessor (`recentlyPlayed`/`mostPlayed`/`continueListening`) is capped/sorted for a specific dashboard section, none of which fit this. New `lib/ui/forgotten_music_page.dart`: header count ("N tracks you haven't heard in N months+"), a `PopupMenuButton` threshold picker (1/3/6/12 months, defaulting to 6 to match the spec's own example), a scrollable list (artwork, title, "last played" or "never played" subtitle, tap-to-play-from-here reusing the `setQueue`+`playAt(startIndex)` pattern every other queue action in this app already uses), and a "Play all" AppBar action. Reached via a new "Forgotten Music" icon button in the Moods page's `AppBar` (`_MoodsPage`, `lib/ui/home_page.dart`) — additive wiring, no change to any existing mood tile's behavior. 15 new pure-logic tests (9 in `test/forgotten_tracks_test.dart` covering never-played-vs-stale-vs-recent classification, the exact threshold boundary, sort order, a custom threshold, an empty library, and stale history entries for tracks no longer owned being correctly ignored; 4 in `test/play_history_store_test.dart`'s new `lastPlayedByTrackId` group) plus 6 widget tests in `test/forgotten_music_page_test.dart` (using the same real-`LibraryStore`/`PlayHistoryStore`-with-fake-path-provider harness `home_dashboard_page_test.dart` already established) covering the default-threshold list, exclusion of a recently-played track, tap-to-play-from-index, "Play all," the threshold popup menu (needed a `pumpAndSettle()` added to this test file's own `_settle` helper — the same popup-menu-animation-timing lesson `playlist_page_test.dart`'s own `_settle` already encodes, initially missing here and caught by a real test failure, not assumed), and the empty-library state. All passed on the first real run except that one `_settle` timing fix. Full suite: 973-974 passing (was 955; the count itself varied by ±1 run-to-run for the unrelated reason below), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. Recommendations' remaining gap is unchanged from above — every other named algorithm plus a provider-neutral framework. **Full-suite flakiness check, done deliberately before committing rather than assumed**: 3 consecutive full-suite runs showed 2 failures and 1 clean pass, all 3 with the *identical* single failure when it occurred — `test/widget_test.dart`'s "AppSettings persists theme presets and builds themed surfaces," always the same `google_fonts` async-gap stack trace documented earlier in this log (`OmnisTheme.build` → `GoogleFonts.interTextTheme`'s fire-and-forget network fetch, which always fails under `TestWidgetsFlutterBinding` and leaks into whichever test happens to be running when it settles). Running `test/widget_test.dart` alone passed cleanly all 8 tests every time, confirming the failure is cross-file bleed during the full suite's run, not a defect in that file or a regression from this increment's new test files. This is the same pre-existing flake this log has documented multiple times before; a 2/3 hit rate here (vs. previously-observed lower frequency) doesn't point at anything in `forgotten_tracks.dart`/`forgotten_music_page.dart`/`lastPlayedByTrackId` specifically — none of them touch `OmnisTheme`/`google_fonts` at all — and is recorded honestly here rather than downplayed, but doesn't block this otherwise-correct, independently-tested increment from shipping. A sixth named algorithm, "Similar Artist," closed 2026-08-16 — this entry's own text already named it first among the still-missing algorithms. New `lib/core/artist_similarity.dart`: `buildArtistProfile(artist, tracks)` aggregates one artist's genre *union* (deliberate opt-in metadata, so any genre any of their tracks carries counts), most-frequent mood (ties broken alphabetically for determinism), and average BPM (only over tracks that have one — a track with none is excluded, not treated as `0`) from whatever tracks the caller supplies; `artistSimilarityScore(a, b)` is the artist-level sibling of `track_similarity.dart`'s `similarityScore` — same renormalize-over-present-signals contract, same "0.0 when nothing comparable, never NaN" guarantee — but genre/mood/BPM only (no key signal at artist granularity, since key is a per-track property with no meaningful artist-level average); `findSimilarArtists(seedArtist, tracksByArtist, {limit})` ranks every other artist above a `0.0` score, excluding the seed and any artist mapped to zero tracks, returning an empty list for a seed not present in the map at all. Wired into `library_page.dart`'s Artists-view rows (both the nested `ExpansionTile` form when albums are grouped beneath an artist, and the flat form when they aren't) as a new "Similar artists" icon opening a `SimpleDialog` of ranked names — tapping one queues and plays that artist's tracks. Deliberately placed inside the `ExpansionTile`'s `title` `Row`, not its `trailing` slot — `ExpansionTile.trailing` fully replaces the widget's own built-in expand/collapse chevron rather than sitting alongside it, which would have silently removed that row's only visual "this expands" affordance. The artist grouping this feature looks up mirrors `buildLibrarySections`'s own Artists-view logic exactly (respecting `groupArtistsByAlbumArtist`), so "the artist" a lookup resolves always matches whatever's actually shown as one row in that view. 25 new tests in `test/artist_similarity_test.dart` (profile aggregation including the mood tie-break and BPM-missing-values exclusion, genre/mood/BPM scoring in isolation and combined, score symmetry, a bounds sweep confirming the score never leaves `[0, 1]`, seed-exclusion, seed-not-present/no-comparable-data/empty-map/empty-track-list edge cases, ranking order, and `limit`) — two real test-writing bugs caught and fixed before shipping, not production bugs: two fixture expectations forgot that when only one signal is present, its raw (not weight-multiplied) score *is* the whole renormalized result, since the weight cancels out in the division — corrected by computing the expected values the same way the function itself does, then re-deriving them by hand rather than trusting the first guess. `library_page.dart`'s own UI wiring has no widget-level test, same standing precedent as `_playSimilar`/every other per-row action on that page — verified via `flutter analyze` plus the full suite staying green. Full suite: 1267 passing (was 1242), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. Recommendations' remaining named gaps: Daily/Weekly Mix, Discovery, Deep Cuts, New Releases, Energy Flow, plus a provider-neutral recommendation framework beyond the existing `IQueueBuilder` fan-out. A seventh named algorithm, "Deep Cuts," closed 2026-08-16 — spec §36's Discovery Engine "Play Something" list names it, and this entry's own text already listed it among the still-missing algorithms. New `QueuePresetPlugin._buildDeepCuts` (`Omnis-Plugins`): resolves the listener's favorited track ids via `IFavoritesProvider.favoriteIds()`, maps them to their artists to get a "favorite artists" set, groups every track in the library by artist, then within each favorite artist's own track group ranks by `IPlayHistoryProvider.playCountFor` and excludes the top ~30% (minimum 1) as "the hits" — the rest are the deep-cut candidates. Deliberately per-artist, not one library-wide play-count cutoff: a favorite artist with 3 tracks and one with 30 shouldn't compete on the same absolute scale, since "the deep cuts" is inherently a claim about one artist's own catalog shape. Requires **both** `IFavoritesProvider` and `IPlayHistoryProvider` — missing either degrades to empty, the same contract `_buildRediscover` already established for a two-provider preset; a favorite artist with too few tracks for a meaningful hits/deep-cuts split (every track ranks as "a hit") contributes nothing rather than an arbitrary pick. Both interfaces already existed with the right shape in `packages/omnis_plugin_api` — no plugin-api version bump needed, only the `omnis_plugins` package tag moved. 10 new tests in `Omnis-Plugins/test/queue_preset_plugin_test.dart` (missing-either-provider no-ops, nothing-favorited, too-few-tracks-for-a-split contributes nothing, a genuine hit/deep-cut split, a non-favorite artist's tracks never surfacing alongside a real favorite, multi-artist independence proving the split is per-artist not library-wide, a multi-artist collab track counted only once, the default 50-item limit, case-insensitive query matching) — plus one pre-existing stale test fixed (`supportedQueries lists exactly the seven presets` updated to eight, now including "Deep Cuts"). All new tests passed on the first real run. `Omnis-Plugins` suite: 469 passing (was 459), tagged `v0.31.0`, pushed. Cross-repo version bump: this repo's `pubspec.yaml` `omnis_plugins` ref bumped `v0.30.0` → `v0.31.0` (hit the same transient pub git-cache ref-lock error this log has documented before — cleared the stale cached clone under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Pub\Cache\git`, not a real dependency problem); clean-clone resolution re-verified. Main-repo full suite: 1274 passing (unchanged — this increment adds no main-repo tests, only `Omnis-Plugins` ones), `flutter analyze` clean in both repos. Recommendations' remaining named gaps: Daily/Weekly Mix, Discovery, New Releases, Energy Flow, plus a provider-neutral recommendation framework. |
| 40 | Sonic similarity | 🟡 Partial — `AudioAnalysisPlugin` extracts real acoustic features (BPM/key/mood/genre) via Essentia. Closed 2026-08-15: real distance/similarity computation now exists (`lib/core/track_similarity.dart`, see item 39's "Similar Track" entry) over those same discrete features — genre Jaccard, exact mood/key match, linear BPM falloff, weighted and renormalized to whatever signals both tracks actually have. Deliberately not a real acoustic-fingerprint/embedding system (would need real signal-processing infrastructure, the same reasoning `metadata_enrichment_plugin.dart` gives for not attempting a from-scratch Essentia integration) — an honestly-scoped feature-distance metric over existing data, not a claim of audio-content matching. |
| 41 | Radio | 🟡 Partial (was genuine 0%, closed 2026-08-13) — new `RadioPlugin` (`Omnis-Plugins`) is a real client to the free, keyless [Radio Browser](https://www.radio-browser.info) directory (search/top-stations/by-tag, all real HTTP calls against the real public API), converting stations to ordinary `BaseTrack`s (`type: TrackType.radio`) that play through the existing engine with zero special-casing — a station's `streamUrl` is just an Icecast/Shoutcast stream URL, the same path YouTube/Spotify tracks already use. New "Radio" tab (`lib/ui/radio_page.dart`) with search + a top-stations default view. The recently-played half of the "no favorites/recently-played integration for stations" gap closed 2026-08-14: `MainCore` genuinely called `PlayHistoryStore.recordPlay()` for a played station all along, but `HomeDashboardPage` joined every history entry against `LibraryRepository`'s *scanned* library purely by id — a station (fetched live from Radio Browser, never imported) was never part of that library, so the entry existed but was silently invisible. Root cause turned out broader than radio specifically: the identical gap applies to *any* played track type that isn't locally scanned (Spotify/YouTube/Jellyfin/Plex/Subsonic/DLNA/Emby all share it too), so the fix is general, not radio-only. New `TrackPlayStats.trackSnapshot` — a full `BaseTrack.toJson()` captured by `PlayHistoryStore.recordPlay()` at record time, but only when `track.type != TrackType.local` (a local track's full metadata already lives in the scanned library, so a second copy would be pure duplication) — gives `HomeDashboardPage`'s join a fallback: library lookup first, snapshot reconstruction (`BaseTrack.fromJson`, wrapped so a corrupted snapshot skips just that one entry rather than crashing the whole dashboard load, the same per-entry-defensive stance every JSON-backed store in this app already holds) second. 6 new tests: 5 in `test/play_history_store_test.dart` (a non-local track gets a snapshot, a local one doesn't, the snapshot round-trips through JSON, a pre-existing record with no `trackSnapshot` field decodes as `null` rather than throwing, `recordPosition` preserves an existing snapshot instead of wiping it) and 1 in `test/home_dashboard_page_test.dart` proving a played-but-never-scanned radio station genuinely renders in Recently Played/Most Played. All passed on the first real run. Full suite: 702 passing (was 696), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. The *favorites* half closed 2026-08-14 too: `radio_page.dart` now has a real heart-icon toggle per station (filled when favorited, matching `library_page.dart`'s own `_isFavorite`/`_toggleFavorite` pattern exactly, down to the same "Favorites plugin disabled" message rather than a crash when that plugin isn't registered), backed by the same `FavoritesPlugin` every other favoritable track already uses — a station's `BaseTrack.id` (`radio:$uuid`) is just another id to that plugin, no station-specific storage needed. 3 new tests in `test/radio_page_test.dart`: favoriting fills the heart and persists to the real plugin, un-favoriting round-trips it back, and a missing/disabled Favorites plugin shows a message instead of crashing. All passed on the first real run. Deliberately scoped narrower than a full end-to-end fix: the Playlists page's aggregate "Favorites" smart list (`playlist_page.dart`'s `_favorites?.favoritesFrom(_libraryTracks)`) only searches `LibraryRepository`'s scanned library, which a radio station is never part of — so a favorited station is genuinely marked favorite (toggle state, `isFavorite()` all correct) but still won't appear in that aggregate list, the identical "recorded but not surfaced" shape as the history-visibility gap closed the same day, just not fixed here since doing so honestly would need a real snapshot-storage addition to `FavoritesPlugin` itself (it currently persists only ids, no track data) — real, separate work, not attempted in this increment. Custom/manually-added stream URL entry closed 2026-08-15: new `lib/core/custom_radio_station_store.dart` (`CustomRadioStation`/`CustomRadioStationStore`, the same atomic-write + schema-versioned shape every other store here uses) persists a user-entered name/URL pair and converts it to an ordinary `BaseTrack` (`type: TrackType.radio`) the same shape `RadioPlugin`'s own Radio Browser conversion already produces — no `AudioEngine` or `RadioPlugin` change needed, since favoriting/queueing/play-history all key off `BaseTrack.id` generically and a custom station's id (`radio:custom:<timestamp>`) is just another id to every one of those. `radio_page.dart` gained an "Add station" AppBar action (name + stream URL dialog, validated as a real http(s) URL with a non-empty host before persisting — not verified reachable, the same "trust what's entered, fail at play time if it's wrong" stance a Radio Browser-fetched URL already gets) and a "My stations" section above the existing Top-stations/Search-results list, each row getting the same favorite-heart toggle fetched stations already have plus a delete action. Still gaps: single fixed API mirror, not the full DNS-round-robin server discovery Radio Browser recommends at production scale. |
| 42 | Smart playlists | 🟡 Partial (real rule engine closed 2026-08-14, was a genuine 0% for it despite the name) — new `SmartPlaylistRule`/`RuleCondition` model (`Omnis-Plugins`, `smart_playlist_rule.dart`) gives `SmartPlaylistPlugin` a real ALL/ANY/NONE, multi-condition engine over title/artist/album/genre/mood/year/rating, additive to (not replacing) the existing flat mood-string matcher that still serves the Moods tab's ephemeral `IQueueBuilder` flow. A saved rule's membership is recomputed fresh against the current library every time it's played, unlike `PlaylistStore`'s fixed-track-id playlists. The `rating:` condition reaches `RatingsPlugin` through a new `IRatingsProvider` capability interface (`packages/omnis_plugin_api`, `plugin-api-v0.15.0`) rather than a direct cross-plugin type dependency — proven with a real `PluginManager`/`ServiceRegistry` integration test (`test/smart_playlist_ratings_integration_test.dart`), not just a caller-supplied fake lookup. UI lives on the plugin's own settings page: create a named rule, play it, edit it in place, delete it. Rule *editing* closed 2026-08-14 — tapping a saved rule's new Edit button repopulates the same create-form (name, match type, every condition row) from that rule and switches it into an "Edit smart playlist" mode; saving reuses the rule's existing id (`SmartPlaylistPlugin.saveRule` already replaces-by-id) rather than minting a new one, so it's a genuine in-place update, not a second entry — no new plugin-level method needed, purely a UI-state change plus a "Cancel" escape hatch back to create mode. Deleting the rule currently being edited also exits edit mode rather than leaving the form silently pointed at an id that no longer exists. 24 tests for the rule model, 10 for the plugin's persistence layer (both per-entry-defensive-decode, matching every other JSON-backed store in this app), 3 cross-plugin integration tests, and — the first widget-level test coverage for any plugin's settings UI anywhere in `Omnis-Plugins` (every other plugin's settings page there is `flutter analyze`-verified only) — 4 new `testWidgets` tests reaching the private settings widget through `uiSlot('plugin_settings')` the same way `plugin_settings_page_test.dart` does in the main repo, covering edit-populates-the-form, save-updates-in-place, cancel-discards, and delete-while-editing. Surfacing on the main Playlists page closed 2026-08-15: `playlist_page.dart` gained a new "Smart playlists" section (shown whenever `SmartPlaylistPlugin` is enabled, listing every saved rule with its match-type/condition-count summary, e.g. "ALL of 3 conditions") sitting alongside the existing Current queue/Queue history/Favorites/Recently played/Most played smart entries — tapping a rule calls the same `buildQueueForRule` + `setQueue` + `play` sequence the plugin's own settings page already uses, and a delete action calls `deleteRule` directly. Deliberately did **not** duplicate the create/edit rule builder here: a "Manage" link deep-links to the plugin's existing settings page (`PluginSettingsPage`, looked up via `PluginManager.byId('smart_playlist')`) for that, refreshing this page's own list on return — one real rule-editing UI, not two to keep in sync. 5 new tests in `test/playlist_page_test.dart`'s new `Smart playlists on the Playlists page (item 42)` group (no section when the plugin isn't registered, an empty-state message when registered with zero rules, a saved rule's name/summary rendering, tapping a rule actually building and playing a real queue against a real scanned-library fixture, deleting a rule removing it from both the list and the plugin's own persistence), all passing on the first real run. The `equals`/other string-operator gap closed 2026-08-15: the builder's operator dropdown for a string field (title/artist/album/genre/mood) now offers `equals` alongside `contains` — `RuleCondition.matches` always evaluated it correctly (case-insensitive exact match), the restriction was purely `_operatorsFor()`'s hard-coded operator list in the UI, not a model gap. A `favorite` condition closed 2026-08-15: `RuleField.favorite` reaches `FavoritesPlugin` through the same `IFavoritesProvider` capability interface item 39's "Favorites Mix" preset introduced the same day, threaded through `RuleCondition.matches`/`SmartPlaylistRule.apply`/`SmartPlaylistPlugin.buildQueueForRule` exactly like `rating:`'s existing `ratingOf` lookup — proven with 2 more real `PluginManager`/`ServiceRegistry` integration tests in `test/smart_playlist_ratings_integration_test.dart`, not just unit-level fake lookups. Only `equals` applies to a boolean field; the builder UI swaps the free-text value `TextField` for a "Favorited"/"Not favorited" `DropdownButton` when a condition's field is Favorite, defaulting its value to `"true"` the instant the row switches into it so the visible dropdown and the saved value can never disagree (and so a freshly-added Favorite condition is never silently dropped by the existing "skip empty-value conditions on save" rule). Still missing: no persistence as a real `PlaylistStore` entry, no import/export. BPM/duration/bitrate/codec conditions closed 2026-08-16 — the comparison doc's own "conditions not yet in `RuleField`" list names play count/skip count/BPM/key/duration/bitrate/sample rate/codec/file size/path/folder/file type/energy/loudness/replay gain/lyrics available/artwork available/custom tags; this increment deliberately closes only the four fields that read straight off `BaseTrack` with zero new provider/lookup needed — `bpm`, `duration`, `bitrateKbps`, `codec` — the same fields `library_search.dart`'s own `bpm:`/`bitrate:`/`format:` search qualifiers already read directly, confirmed by reading that file's matcher cases before adding the smart-playlist equivalents. Play count/skip count/energy/loudness/replay gain need a new capability interface or unbuilt data (bigger, separate work); path/folder/file type/custom tags are separate asks, not attempted here. New `RuleField.bpm`/`.duration`/`.bitrate` get the full numeric comparison set (equals/`>=`/`<=`/`>`/`<`), the same operators `year`/`rating` already support — `bpm` needed its own `_matchesDouble` helper (structurally identical to `_matchesNumber` but for a `double?` field, since `int.tryParse` and `double.tryParse` are different calls). New `RuleField.codec` only ever uses `equals`, exact and case-insensitive — a new `_matchesCodec` method, not `_matchesString`, since a codec label like "FLAC"/"MP3" is categorical, not free text, the same "contains doesn't mean anything for this" stance `favorite`/`thumbUp`/`thumbDown` already established for their own equals-only boolean fields; `format:mp` must not match "MP3" here any more than it does in `library_search.dart`. `_fieldLabel`/`_operatorsFor` in the builder UI extended accordingly (BPM/Duration (seconds)/Bitrate (kbps)/Format), reusing the exact numeric-vs-equals-only branch shape already there. 16 new tests in `Omnis-Plugins/test/smart_playlist_rule_test.dart` and 5 widget tests in `smart_playlist_plugin_edit_test.dart` (field-dropdown offering the right operator set, saving building the correct condition) — one real test-infrastructure issue found and fixed while writing the widget tests, not a production bug: `DropdownButton`'s own popup menu is a lazily-built `ListView` that, with the RuleField enum now 14 entries long, didn't render "BPM"/"Duration"/"Bitrate"/"Format" into the tree at all when they sat past the menu's initial cache extent (confirmed via a throwaway debug harness comparing the actually-built menu items with and without a pre-existing saved rule taking up page space above it) — fixed with a direct `tester.drag` on the popup's own `ListView` before searching for those items, not just an `ensureVisible`/`dragUntilVisible` against the wrong scrollable (which itself threw, since `.last` didn't reliably resolve to the popup's own list among several `Scrollable`s in the tree). All new tests passed after that one fix. `Omnis-Plugins` suite: 490 passing (was 469), tagged `v0.32.0`, pushed. Main-repo full suite: 1274 passing (unchanged — this increment adds no main-repo tests, only `Omnis-Plugins` ones), `flutter analyze` clean in both repos. Import/export closed 2026-08-16 — item 42's own remaining named gap. New pure `exportRulesToJson(List<SmartPlaylistRule>)`/`importRulesFromJson(String)` in `smart_playlist_rule.dart`: a small `{"schemaVersion":1,"rules":[...]}` envelope reusing each rule's own existing `toJson()`/`fromJson()`; import is per-entry defensive (one malformed rule in a pasted payload is skipped, not fatal to the rest) and degrades to an empty list — never throws — for malformed JSON or a non-envelope-shaped payload. Thin `SmartPlaylistPlugin.exportRulesJson()`/`importRulesJson(String)` wrappers; import adds-or-replaces by id, the same semantics `saveRule` already has, returning the real count actually imported. UI uses the **clipboard**, not a file — `Omnis-Plugins` has no `file_picker` dependency, and adding one just for this would be a heavier lift than the feature needs: a new "Export" button (disabled with no saved rules) shows the JSON in a `SelectableText` dialog with "Copy to clipboard"; a new "Import" button opens a paste `TextField` dialog and reports how many rules were actually imported, or an honest "No valid smart playlists found" for a bad paste. 7 new tests in `smart_playlist_rule_test.dart`, 5 in `smart_playlist_plugin_test.dart`, 4 widget tests in `smart_playlist_plugin_edit_test.dart` — two real bugs found and fixed while writing the widget tests, not assumed away: (1) this test environment doesn't wire up a default `Clipboard` mock the way it does for other platform channels — a real `Clipboard.setData`/`getData` call hung indefinitely (confirmed via a minimal standalone repro) rather than failing fast, fixed with an explicit `TestDefaultBinaryMessengerBinding` mock handler for `SystemChannels.platform`; (2) a genuine app-code bug, not a test artifact: the Import dialog's `TextEditingController` was disposed synchronously right after `showDialog` returned, but that Future completes as soon as `Navigator.pop` is *called*, not once the route's exit transition finishes — disposing that early could race a still-animating `TextField`/`InputDecorator` still listening to the same controller, a real "used after dispose" crash `flutter analyze` never would have caught, fixed by deferring disposal one frame via `WidgetsBinding.instance.addPostFrameCallback`. All tests passed after both fixes. `Omnis-Plugins` suite: 506 passing (was 490), tagged `v0.33.0`, pushed. | 43 | AI | 🟡 Partial (was genuine 0%, closed 2026-08-13) — real `IAIProvider` capability interface added (`packages/omnis_plugin_api`), and a first real implementation: `AIPlaylistPlugin` (`Omnis-Plugins`) builds a playlist from a natural-language prompt using Anthropic's Messages API (user-supplied key, never embedded) — the spec's §21 "Playlist creation" example ("Make me a two-hour workout playlist") almost verbatim. The model is given a compact JSON summary of the real library and told to reply with only a JSON array of track ids; every returned id is checked against the library before use, so the model can never inject a track that doesn't actually exist. 14 new tests against a mocked HTTP client, including one proving a fabricated id is silently dropped and one proving a markdown-code-fence-wrapped response (a common LLM habit despite instructions) still parses. Still genuine 0% for every other AI feature the spec names as part of this "major optional ecosystem": natural language search, metadata cleanup, tagging, recommendations, a conversational library assistant, voice control, and artist-similarity discovery. Library sample sent to the model is capped at 300 tracks — a real, documented limit, not full-library coverage. **Not exercised against the real Anthropic API** — protocol-level correctness only, no live call with real spend attached. Natural language search closed 2026-08-16 — the one small, self-contained slice of that still-0% list; the others (conversational assistant, voice control, tagging) are materially bigger, separate asks. New `IAIProvider.searchLibrary(query, library)` (`packages/omnis_plugin_api`, tagged `plugin-api-v0.20.0`) — distinct from `buildPlaylistFromPrompt`: a search returns whatever genuinely matches in no particular order, not a curated listening sequence, same never-invents-a-track/never-throws contract. `AIPlaylistPlugin` implements it by extracting the shared Anthropic-request/response machinery both methods now need into a new private `_queryModel({library, systemPrompt, userMessagePrefix})` — a pure refactor of the pre-existing `buildPlaylistFromPrompt` body first (re-ran its full existing 15-test suite unmodified before adding anything, all still passing, confirming zero behavior change), then both methods became thin callers differing only in their system prompt (curated order vs. genuine-match-only) and guard-clause wording. New "Search your library" section in the plugin's settings UI, its own result list and "Play" action separate from playlist generation's — a search finding matches isn't the same claim as building a listening order, so it gets its own queue action rather than being folded into the existing results state. 6 new tests in `Omnis-Plugins/test/ai_playlist_plugin_test.dart`'s new `searchLibrary` group (blank-query/no-key/empty-library guards, real request shape proving the search-specific system prompt is actually sent — not the playlist one reused by mistake, id-mapping/never-invents, and an empty-match array being a valid non-error result). All passed on the first real run — no bug found, a genuine feature-completion. `Omnis-Plugins` suite: 531 passing (was 525), tagged `v0.38.0`, pushed. ## Phase 7 — Advanced UX

> **Audited 2026-08-13.** Full findings in [OMNIS_2_0_MISSED_DEEP_PHASE.md](OMNIS_2_0_MISSED_DEEP_PHASE.md#phase-7--advanced-ux). Themes/layouts/car-mode/accessibility are far more built than the blanket tracker credited; TV mode, OS widgets, and general automation are genuine 0%.

| 44 | Themes | 🟡 Partial — a real declarative theme engine (import from URL/file, closed-schema colors/typography/shape/motion) exists. Named-preset parity closed 2026-08-15: confirmed genuinely unbuilt by grepping both repos for `AppThemePreset.pure`/`.drive`/`.future`/`.audiophile`/`.karaoke` — zero hits; `AppThemePreset` only had `{classic, midnight, aurora, sunset}`, three invented substitutes with no connection to the spec's own 6 named presets (`docs/OMNIS_2_0_UI_SPEC.md` §22-27: Classic/Pure/Drive/Karaoke/Future/Audiophile). Replaced `midnight`/`aurora`/`sunset` with `pure`/`drive`/`karaoke`/`future`/`audiophile` in the enum (`classic` unchanged, still accent-color-driven rather than a fixed palette) — a straight rename/replace, not additive, since the spec's named set is meant to *be* the polished set rather than three extra invented options sitting alongside it. Five new fixed color pairs in `OmnisPalette` (`lib/ui/theme/omnis_colors.dart`): Pure (near-monochrome grey, letting artwork/typography carry the visual weight), Drive (bold saturated red/amber, legible at a glance — the same reasoning automotive UIs lean on warning-adjacent colors), Karaoke (warm gold/pink, a lyric-focused interface reading warmer than a plain playback one), Future (cool blue/teal — deliberately restrained, the spec explicitly warns against a "neon cyberpunk cliché"), Audiophile (desaturated blue-grey, reading like a measurement tool). Wired into `OmnisTheme.build`'s existing `presetColors` switch (unchanged mechanism — `ColorScheme.fromSeed` off each pair, same as the presets it replaces) and the Appearance settings page's preset dropdown. Deliberately scoped to naming/palette parity only — this does **not** touch navigation/Home/Library layout composition the spec's own "theme = composition" concept demands (Classic = sidebar, Future = floating radial nav, Drive = bottom nav, Karaoke = top controls, Audiophile = left technical nav); that's a materially larger, separate system already tracked under item 45, not attempted here. Also fixed a stale doc comment in `lib/ui/theme/declarative/theme_manager.dart` claiming "four built-in presets" (now six). Tests: extended the one pre-existing test in `test/widget_test.dart` that touches `OmnisTheme.build` (kept deliberately last in that file for the documented google_fonts-under-`flutter_test` reason) with a persistence round-trip loop over all six `AppThemePreset.values` — cheap, no new `OmnisTheme.build` calls — rather than adding a second dedicated test file that would call it again, the same "don't multiply the known-fragile call sites" discipline this log has followed since the high-contrast increment. New `lib/core/home_layout_store.dart`: `homeSectionCatalog` (a stable id → display-name map, independent of the title so a future rename can't silently orphan a saved layout), `HomeLayoutStore` (the usual atomic-write + schema-versioned-envelope shape every store here already uses), and a pure `applyHomeLayout(defaultSections, saved, idOf)` — an empty `saved` returns the default sections completely unchanged (existing users see zero behavior change until they opt in), a section marked invisible is dropped, a saved section with nothing to show right now is silently skipped (matches this app's already-existing "section absent when empty" behavior), and a section the saved layout never mentions at all (a new section type added after the user last customized) is appended at the end, visible by default, rather than silently vanishing. `_HomeSection` gained a stable `id` field distinct from its display title. New "Customize" AppBar action opens `_HomeCustomizeSheet` — all six known sections listed regardless of current data (so a user can pre-arrange an empty section), `ReorderableListView` drag-to-reorder plus a `CheckboxListTile` per section for visibility, "Reset" (clears the saved layout back to default) and "Done" (only writes when something actually changed). 11 new tests in `test/home_layout_store_test.dart` (store round-trip, corrupt-JSON/malformed-single-record tolerance, `clear()`; `applyHomeLayout`'s unchanged-when-empty/reorder/hide/skip-missing/append-new-section/empty-defaults cases) and 4 new widget tests in `test/home_dashboard_page_test.dart` (defaults-all-visible, uncheck-and-Done hides a section and survives a reload, Reset restores default, Done-with-no-changes writes nothing) — **three real test-infrastructure issues found and fixed, not production bugs**: (1) `HomeLayoutStore`'s cached file handle (the same "resolves once, a fresh temp dir per test only matters for the first test" behavior `LibraryStore`/`PlayHistoryStore` already document) meant a test writing malformed JSON directly to its own per-test temp-dir path was writing somewhere the store itself never read from — fixed by switching to `setUpAll` for the path provider (one stable temp dir for the whole file, matching `custom_radio_station_store_test.dart`'s own established pattern) so direct `File(...)` writes and the store's own reads agree on the same path; (2) the Customize sheet's "Done"/"Reset" buttons could render below the test viewport once six sections plus the header exceeded 600px, first surfacing as an off-screen `tap()` warning — fixed by making the whole sheet body scroll together (`SingleChildScrollView` wrapping a `shrinkWrap`+`NeverScrollableScrollPhysics` `ReorderableListView`, so drag-to-reorder's long-press gesture stays distinct from the outer scroll) plus `tester.ensureVisible` before each tap, the same established fix this session has already applied to other off-screen dropdown/button cases; (3) a modal bottom sheet's own exit animation isn't necessarily complete after a single settle pump — while still mid-dismiss, its own now-hidden "Recently Added" checkbox label transiently still matched a `find.text` check meant for the dashboard underneath, giving a false result; fixed with an explicit `tester.pumpAndSettle()` after closing the sheet. All tests passed after those three fixes. Full suite: 1352 passing (was 1337), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. Two real Flutter key-handling gotchas surfaced only by writing genuine `tester.sendKeyEvent` tests, not assumed from the API: (1) `CallbackShortcuts` never requests focus itself, so with nothing else focused in a touch-first app these bindings would silently never fire at all — fixed with a fallback anchor `FocusNode` claimed only when nothing more specific already holds focus, itself needing a one-frame-deferred claim (verified via a real nested-autofocus race test) so a legitimate descendant `autofocus: true` widget (a dialog's `TextField`) always still wins; (2) ordinary character input, including Space, isn't reliably marked "handled" by a focused `TextField` the way arrow-key cursor movement is — left unguarded, typing a space while searching the Library would have inserted the character *and* toggled playback — fixed with an explicit `_typingInTextField` check ahead of every binding. 11 new tests in `test/global_keyboard_shortcuts_test.dart` (every binding fires a real fake-`AudioEngine` call, not just renders; the disabled-via-settings and focused-TextField cases are both covered), 2 more in `test/settings_page_test.dart` (category navigation + search deep-link). New `lib/core/text_scale.dart` (`clampTextScale`, a pure 0.85-1.5 clamp — below that text becomes hard to read, above it common layouts like button rows start clipping, the same reasoning `albumArtScale`'s own 0.7-1.4 clamp already applies to a different visual scale) backs a new `AppSettings.textScaleFactor` (default `1.0`, clamped on write so a persisted value can never end up outside the safe range even from a non-UI writer). Wired into `lib/main.dart`'s `MaterialApp` via a new `builder:` param — `MediaQuery(data: ...copyWith(textScaler: TextScaler.linear(clampTextScale(...))))` wrapping the whole app, the same "intercept via MediaQuery" mechanism Flutter itself uses for OS-level text scaling, just user- rather than OS-controlled; this is the app's first use of `MaterialApp.builder` at all. New "Text size" row on the Accessibility page's existing "Display" section (alongside High contrast) — a `Slider` (0.85-1.5, 13 divisions, live percentage label) plus a static preview line; because the scaling applies via the top-level `MediaQuery` wrapper, the *entire* settings page (not just the preview line) actually rescales live as the slider moves, giving stronger real-time feedback than the preview line alone would. Wired into Settings search (`lib/ui/settings_page.dart`) the same way every other Accessibility toggle already is. 4 new tests in `test/text_scale_test.dart` (in-range passthrough, below/above-range clamping, exact boundary values), 1 in `test/widget_test.dart` (default/persistence/out-of-range-clamps-on-write, placed before the file's own `OmnisTheme.build`-touching test so it doesn't disturb that test's documented "must stay last" ordering), and 4 in `test/accessibility_settings_page_test.dart` (slider renders at the persisted default, dragging to the max end persists `1.5`, and the `highlightField: 'text_size'` deep-link doesn't throw). `lib/main.dart`'s new `MaterialApp.builder` itself has no direct test coverage — no test constructs `OmnisApp` directly (it already touches `OmnisTheme.build`/`google_fonts` regardless of this change, the same pre-existing test-infrastructure dead end documented above for high-contrast mode), verified instead via `flutter analyze` clean plus the full suite staying green. All new tests passed on the first real run. Full suite: 1073 passing (was 1065), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. Confirmed genuinely missing by reading `global_keyboard_shortcuts.dart` in full (play/pause, next/prev, seek, volume up/down — no mute binding anywhere) and grepping both repos case-insensitively for `mute` (only unrelated hits: `omnis_colors.dart`'s `mutedColor` theming field). `AudioEngine` has no separate mute concept, only a continuous `volume`/`setVolume`, so new pure `lib/core/mute_toggle.dart` (`toggleMute(currentVolume, volumeBeforeMute)`) treats "mute" as "drive volume to 0.0, remembering the prior value to restore on the next press" rather than a real boolean flag — deliberately, since a bare flag could drift out of sync with the live volume if it's changed some other way (e.g. the user drags the volume slider while "muted"); this toggle always reads whatever the engine's volume genuinely is. Unmuting with nothing ever remembered (volume started at 0 and this toggle was never actually used to mute) falls back to a full `1.0` rather than leaving the user stuck silent with no way out via the same key. New `M` binding in `GlobalKeyboardShortcuts` (a plain letter key, not a platform media-mute constant — `LogicalKeyboardKey` has no verified-cross-platform `mediaMute` equivalent to `mediaPlayPause`/`mediaTrackNext`), a `_volumeBeforeMute` field mirroring `_adjustVolume`'s existing shape, and a new "M — Mute / unmute" row in `keyboard_settings_page.dart`'s reference list. 8 new tests in `test/mute_toggle_test.dart` (mute remembers and zeroes, unmute restores exactly, unmute-with-nothing-remembered falls back to 1.0, a full mute-then-unmute round trip, several alternating presses, volume changed externally while at 0 doesn't crash, volume raised externally between presses is treated as a fresh mute not stale state, a remembered exact-0.0 value treated the same as nothing remembered) and 2 widget tests extending `test/global_keyboard_shortcuts_test.dart`'s existing real-fake-`AudioEngine` pattern (M mutes a non-zero volume; a second M press restores the exact value the first one muted). All passed on the first real run — no bug found, a genuine feature-completion. Full suite: 1183 passing (was 1173), `flutter analyze` clean. fixed by switching to `<Space>`, which is on that list. **Verification gap, documented rather than papered over**: after the fix, re-pinning the widget to confirm it visually (live text, working buttons) could not be completed — `adb shell input draganddrop`/manual `motionevent` DOWN-hold-MOVE-UP sequences against this Compose-based Pixel Launcher widget picker succeeded exactly once (which is how the `<View>` bug was found in the first place, via the resulting "Can't load widget" error) and could not be reliably reproduced across roughly a dozen further attempts with varied timing/technique — a testing-tool/gesture-automation limitation, not a reproduced app defect; the fix itself (`<View>` → `<Space>`) is the standard, well-documented resolution for that exact Android exception. | 2026-08-12 | 3, 4 | Recovery, Database | Found and fixed two related concurrency bugs while re-reading the stores just hardened above: (1) `RecoveryJournal.save()`/`clear()` had no serialization — two calls close together (a pause and the 20s heartbeat) raced on the same `.tmp` path. (2) Worse: `PlayHistoryStore.recordPlay`/`recordPosition` each do their own full load→mutate→save cycle, and `MainCore` fires both unawaited from independent stream listeners around every track change — two concurrent calls could each load the same base state and whichever saved last silently discarded the other's update (a lost play count or lost position, not just a file-write race). Both stores now serialize their operations onto one queue, so each call sees the previous one's committed result. Added regression tests exercising real (unawaited, `Future.wait`-joined) concurrency, not just sequential calls. Full suite: 487 passing, `flutter analyze` clean. |
