Root cause: key-event dispatch only walks *outward* from whatever holds `primaryFocus` through its ancestors, and the app's default focus holder (the current route's `FocusScopeNode`) sits *above*, not below, `GlobalKeyboardShortcuts` in the tree. Fixed with a fallback anchor `FocusNode`, claimed only when `primaryFocus` is still `null`/a bare `FocusScopeNode` — but naively claiming it in the first post-frame callback stole focus from a genuine descendant `autofocus: true` `TextField` in a dedicated race test, because that widget resolves its own autofocus claim via a post-frame callback too, and an ancestor's `initState` (this widget's) always runs, and therefore registers its callback, before a descendant's; deferring the actual claim one additional frame let the real widget's callback settle first, verified by the same test then passing. (2) Even after focus was reaching the widget correctly, a focused `TextField` didn't stop Space from also reaching the global handler — ordinary character input isn't reliably marked "handled" by `EditableText`'s own key handling the way arrow-key cursor movement is (text composition goes through a separate IME channel the `Shortcuts`/`Actions` bubbling model doesn't see), so typing a space while searching the Library would have inserted the character *and* toggled playback. Fixed with an explicit `_typingInTextField` check (walks up from the focused context looking for an ancestor `EditableText`) ahead of every binding, not relying on propagation to stop it. 11 new tests in `test/global_keyboard_shortcuts_test.dart`, 2 more in `test/settings_page_test.dart` (category navigation, search deep-link). Full suite: 647 passing (was 634), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. | 2026-08-14 | 22 | Audio | Extended `AudioFormatReader` with real AIFF/AIFC header parsing — the closest cousin to the already-parsed WAV reader (both simple, linear, chunk-based containers), chosen as the first of the three remaining unparsed formats (M4A/AAC, OGG/Opus, WMA are still extension-only). Walks `FORM`/`COMM` chunks (big-endian, word-aligned exactly like RIFF) for channels/bit-depth/sample-rate, and computes a real PCM bitrate (`sampleRate * channels * bitDepth`) the same way the WAV reader derives one from its `byteRate` field, which AIFF's `COMM` chunk doesn't carry directly. The one genuinely new piece of decoding: AIFF's sample rate is an 80-bit IEEE 754 extended-precision float (explicit, not implicit, leading mantissa bit — unlike ordinary 32/64-bit floats), decoded by a new `_decodeExtended80`. Also distinguishes AIFC (the compressed variant, same container shape plus a `compressionType` field in `COMM`) from plain AIFF via the `FORM` chunk's own type field, and only reports a computed bitrate for AIFC when its `compressionType` is actually uncompressed (`NONE`, or `sowt` — byte-swapped PCM, still uncompressed) — a real compression codec (e.g. `ima4`) still gets real channels/sample-rate/bit-depth but `bitrateKbps` stays `null` rather than reporting a number that would be flatly wrong for it, the same "don't guess what you can't derive honestly" stance the MP3/WAV readers already hold. Two real bugs found purely by writing tests against independently hand-packed bytes (a from-scratch `BigInt`-based 80-bit float *encoder*, not the reader's own decode logic run backwards): (1) an off-by-two byte offset reading `COMM`'s bit-depth/sample-rate fields two bytes past where they actually start (a copy-paste slip from an earlier field-offset calculation), caught when a well-formed test file decoded to all-null fields instead of throwing — the bug was inside the value ranges, not an exception; (2) a missing `'aifc'` case in the format-dispatch `switch`, silently routing every `.aifc` file to the generic "unrecognized extension" fallback instead of the new reader at all — caught the same way, a well-formed AIFC test file also decoding to nothing. 7 new tests in `test/audio_format_reader_test.dart` (plain AIFF, a second sample-rate/bit-depth/channel combination proving the extended-float decode isn't hardcoded to one value, AIFC with `NONE`/`sowt`/`ima4` compression types, a corrupt-file degrade, and `.aif` extension recognition); one pre-existing test updated (it asserted `.aiff`'s *old*, pre-parsing hardcoded label — now genuinely parsed, so that assertion moved into the new group instead). Full suite: 658 passing (was 651), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. |
| 2026-08-14 | 22 | Audio | Extended `AudioFormatReader` to Ogg Vorbis/Opus — the second of the three remaining extension-only formats to get real parsing today (M4A/AAC and WMA remain, judged more work: MP4 box nesting and ASF's GUID-object structure both have more surface area than Ogg's flat page format). Reads the first Ogg page (`OggS` capture pattern, fixed header, segment-table lacing-value summation to get the exact packet length) and parses its payload as the stream's identification-header packet, sniffed by signature (`"\x01vorbis"` vs `"OpusHead"`) rather than trusted from the file extension — so a `.opus` file that's actually Vorbis (or vice versa) still gets correctly identified. Real sample rate and channel count for both; deliberately no bitrate for either, a genuine scope decision rather than an oversight: Vorbis's header "nominal bitrate" field is spec-documented as a non-binding encoder hint, not a real number the way MP3's Xing-derived or FLAC's file-size/duration-derived averages are (computing a real one for Ogg would need the *last* page's granule position, i.e. a backward scan near EOF for variable-sized pages — real, separate work), and Opus's header has no bitrate field to read at all. Opus always reports `48000`Hz regardless of its header's own "input sample rate" field, since that field just records the source material's original rate for reference while the decoder itself always outputs at 48kHz. A well-formed page whose payload matches neither signature (a real Ogg FLAC or Theora stream) degrades to a generic `'Ogg'` label rather than misreporting or crashing. 6 new hand-packed-byte tests (independent `buildOggPage`/`buildVorbisIdHeader`/`buildOpusHead` encoders, not the reader's own logic run backwards) — unlike the AIFF work done earlier today, this one had zero bugs on the first real test run, a genuine data point that the AIFF increment's extra care (the off-by-two field offset, the missing `.aifc` switch case) paid off in getting this file's overall pattern right before repeating it. One pre-existing test updated (it asserted `.ogg`'s *old* pre-parsing hardcoded label, same fix class as AIFF's equivalent stale assertion). Full suite: 672 passing (was 666), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. |

| 2026-08-14 | 29 | Plugin platform | Closed item 29's "no backup-before-update/rollback" gap. Root cause confirmed by reading `PluginInstaller.installFromUrl`: a plugin's install directory name is resolved *deterministically* from its own source URL (`_targetDirName`), so `updatePlugin()` re-installing the same plugin always targets the exact same directory the currently-working version lives in, and `installFromUrl` unconditionally deletes whatever's already there before extracting the new download — a failure partway through (network drop, corrupt zip, an invalid manifest in the new version) previously left that directory empty or partially written, while the plugin's `ManagedPlugin` record stayed in `PluginManager._plugins` with its services already unregistered by `updatePlugin`'s own teardown step: installed in name only, silently broken, no way back short of a manual reinstall. Fixed with three new `PluginInstaller` methods — `backupPluginDirectory` (recursive copy to a location *outside* the plugins root, specifically so `listInstalled()`'s "every folder with an `omnis_plugin.yaml` is an installed plugin" scan never mistakes a backup for a second copy), `restorePluginBackup`, `discardPluginBackup` — and `updatePlugin()` now snapshots before attempting the download, discards the snapshot on success, and on failure restores it and re-registers the plugin from the restored directory (via the same `installFromPath`/`_registerInstalledPlugin` path a normal install uses, so it's a full re-initialization, not just files copied back) before rethrowing the original error. A rollback that itself fails surfaces both failures in one message rather than silently losing the second. 9 new tests, all passing on the first real run: 6 direct unit tests for the three new `PluginInstaller` methods (`test/plugin_installer_test.dart`, including a nested-subdirectory case to prove the copy is genuinely recursive) and 3 in `test/plugin_updates_test.dart` against a new `_FailOnUpdateClient` double that can fail the zip download specifically, after a real prior install already succeeded — verifying the rollback happens, the thrown error names the original failure, and the plugin can be updated successfully again afterward rather than being stuck. Full suite: 691 passing (was 682), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. |

| 2026-08-14 | 28 | Plugin platform | Closed item 28's remaining named gap: no auto-disable/auto-retry on repeated failure. New `PluginManager._checkAutoDisable`, wired as a listener on `PluginSandbox`'s existing health-change notification mechanism (`addHealthListener` in the constructor, `removeHealthListener` in `dispose()`) — every time a new `PluginHealthRecord` is added anywhere, it recomputes each plugin's failure count within a rolling 5-minute window and calls the real `disablePlugin()` (not a shortcut — the actual `disable()` hook fires, `AppSettings` persists the choice, services unregister) for any plugin that's crossed 5 failures and isn't already disabled. Deliberately window-based rather than a true consecutive-since-last-success counter the way `PlaybackWatchdog._consecutiveFailures` already works: `PluginHealthRecord`s are failure-only, there's no "hook succeeded" event anywhere to reset a real consecutive counter against, so a rolling window over actual failure timestamps is the honest signal `PluginSandbox.healthRecords` genuinely offers today. Verified no re-trigger loop: `disablePlugin()` sets `enabled = false` before ever calling the plugin's own `disable()` hook, so even a `disable()` that itself throws (adding yet another health record) can't cause a second `disablePlugin()` call, since the auto-disable check always looks at `plugin.enabled` first. 5 new tests in `test/plugin_architecture_test.dart` (new `auto-disable on repeated failure (item 28)` group, plus a small new `_RecordingHealthyPlugin` test double for the cross-plugin isolation case), manufacturing real failure records the same way the file's pre-existing `resetPlugin` tests already do (`manager.sandbox.run(operation: () => throw StateError(...))`) rather than needing an actually-misbehaving plugin. | 2026-08-14 | 22 | Audio | Extended `AudioFormatReader` to M4A/AAC/ALAC — the third of the four remaining extension-only formats to get real parsing (WMA remains; AIFF and Ogg were closed earlier the same day). The first of the four with genuine box-tree nesting rather than a linear chunk/page format: `_readM4a` scans top-level boxes from the start of the file to find `moov` (not assumed first — some muxers put it after `mdat`, proven by a dedicated test), then walks `moov`→`trak`→`mdia`→`minf`→`stbl`→`stsd` to the first sample entry, picking the `trak` whose `mdia`/`hdlr` declares a `soun` handler so a video track appearing first in the file is correctly skipped (also proven by a dedicated test) rather than assumed to be the first `trak`. The sample entry's legacy QuickTime-compatibility fields (channel count/sample size/sample rate) are read first as a baseline, then overridden with more authoritative values where available: AAC's real sample rate/channel count live in `esds`'s buried `AudioSpecificConfig`, not the legacy fields — a well-documented real-world MP4 quirk (encoders commonly leave the legacy fields as placeholders for `mp4a` specifically), reached by walking a full `ES_Descriptor`→`DecoderConfigDescriptor`→`DecoderSpecificInfo` variable-length-descriptor chain (each descriptor's length is its own base-128 continuation-bit encoding, not a fixed field) down to a non-byte-aligned bitstream read with a small MSB-first bit reader. ALAC's nested `alac` magic-cookie box is far simpler — a fixed 24-byte layout carrying its own authoritative bit depth (a real, meaningful field for a *lossless* codec, unlike AAC), channels, sample rate, and average bitrate directly, no bit-level parsing needed. AAC never reports a `bitDepth`, matching this reader's existing stance for Ogg Vorbis/Opus: lossy formats are decoded at whatever precision the decoder chooses, not a real fixed-width PCM value to report. `esds`'s own average-bitrate field is used when present and nonzero; otherwise a duration-derived fallback (`mdhd`'s timescale/duration against the whole file size) covers it, the same technique FLAC's reader already uses. A missing or malformed `esds`/`alac` cookie falls back to the already-parsed legacy fields rather than losing everything — verified with a dedicated `mp4a`-with-no-`esds` test, not just assumed to degrade gracefully. 6 new hand-packed-byte tests in `test/audio_format_reader_test.dart` (independent `mp4Box`/`buildEsds`/`buildAlacCookie`/`buildM4aFile` encoders composing real nested box structures, not the reader's own logic run backwards), all passing on the first real run — zero bugs found, the second format in a row (after Ogg) where getting the hand-packed-test pattern right up front paid off, against AIFF's two real bugs caught the same way. A bare `.aac` file (an ADTS elementary stream — a different frame-sync format entirely, not an MP4 container, closer in shape to MP3) is deliberately still label-only; real, separate work, not attempted here. Full suite: 739 passing (was 733), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. |
| 2026-08-15 | 22 | Audio | Extended `AudioFormatReader` to WMA/ASF — the fourth and last remaining extension-only format, closing item 22's format-detection half entirely. ASF's own container shape is a flat sequence of GUID-identified objects rather than M4A's nested box tree, and turned out simpler to parse than expected going in: `_readWma` reads the Header Object's own declared size up front (so exactly that many bytes come into memory, never the far larger Data Object holding real audio packets after it) and walks its child objects looking for two: the Stream Properties Object whose *own* Stream Type GUID identifies it as the audio stream — skipping a video stream's Stream Properties Object in the same file rather than assuming the first one found is always audio, proven by a dedicated test — and the File Properties Object, for a Maximum Bitrate fallback. The audio stream's Type-Specific Data is a `WAVEFORMATEX` structure — literally the same structure WAV's own `fmt` chunk already carries, just embedded one GUID-object deeper here — giving real sample rate/channels/bits-per-sample directly, plus `nAvgBytesPerSec` as a real encoder-declared average bitrate, preferred over File Properties' Maximum Bitrate (a ceiling, not an average) preferred over a duration-derived fallback (Play Duration against file size, the same technique FLAC/M4A already use) when neither bitrate field is present. Every ASF integer is little-endian, unlike the big-endian formats elsewhere in this file (AIFF, MP4 box sizes), so this uses its own dedicated `_read*LE` helpers rather than the shared big-endian ones — a real, deliberate distinction, not an inconsistency. A handful of well-known `WAVEFORMATEX` format tags get a specific label (WMA/WMA Pro/WMA Lossless/PCM); an unrecognized one still reports real numeric fields with a generic `WMA (0x....)` label rather than guessing which codec it is, the same "report what's real, label what isn't" stance every reader in this file already holds. GUID constants are written as their literal on-disk mixed-endian byte sequence (Microsoft GUIDs store their first three fields little-endian and the last big-endian) rather than converted from the human-readable dashed form at runtime — the same "fixed magic bytes" convention already used for `fLaC`/`RIFF`/`FORM`/`OggS`. 6 new hand-packed-byte tests in `test/audio_format_reader_test.dart` (independent `asfObject`/`buildFilePropertiesObject`/`buildAudioStreamPropertiesObject`/`buildWmaFile` encoders, not the reader's own logic run backwards), all passing on the first real run — the third format this week (after Ogg and M4A) with zero bugs found, against AIFF's two real bugs caught the same way early in the week. With WMA closed, **every container `AudioFormatReader` recognizes now gets real header parsing** except a bare `.aac` file (an ADTS elementary stream, not a container format at all — a different frame-sync format entirely, closer in shape to MP3 — deliberately out of scope, unchanged from the M4A entry's own note). Full suite: 748 passing (was 742), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. |

| 2026-08-15 | 17 | Tag editor | Closed item 17's remaining gap: spec §20's guided "Music Library Cleanup" report. New `lib/core/library_cleanup_analyzer.dart` — a pure function over already-scanned `BaseTrack`s, the same "work from what's already loaded, no new I/O, no fresh scan" shape `findDuplicateTracks`/`findShortTracks` in `library_page.dart` already use — computing all eight of the spec's named categories: missing artwork (`coverArt` null/blank), inconsistent artists/genres (grouped by normalized album — blank albums excluded from grouping entirely — flagging every track whose primary artist/genre disagrees with another track in the same album), duplicate tracks (reuses the existing title+primary-artist matching), albums missing year (an album counts only when *every* track in it lacks a year — one dated sibling makes the value recoverable, not missing), malformed track numbers (null/non-positive, and only within a real named album — a track number is meaningless without one), duplicate albums (the same album+artist pair appearing under two or more differently-cased/spaced exact strings — grouped first by the *raw* variant so two genuinely different artists' same-named albums are never conflated, then those raw variants are grouped again by their normalized form), and corrupt files — a documented heuristic, not a certainty: a local track whose extension `AudioFormatReader` fully parses today but whose `codec` field is still `null`, which can also just mean the track was scanned before that field existed; this analyzer has no way to distinguish the two without triggering a fresh scan, which it deliberately never does. New `lib/ui/library_cleanup_report_page.dart`: an "Analyze Library" report listing all eight counts (a `LibraryCleanupReport.isClean` getter shows a real "nothing to clean up" state rather than eight zero-rows), each drilling into a detail list. Categories with an obvious direct fix — missing artwork, inconsistent artist/genre spelling, malformed track numbers — delegate straight to `LibraryPage`'s own existing `_editTags` (passed in as a callback, so the real `TagEditorDialog`, its re-read-after-write, and its persistence are reused exactly as-is, not reimplemented a second time); duplicate tracks/albums point at the page's pre-existing "Find duplicates & short tracks…" tool instead of a second merge/delete implementation; corrupt files are listed read-only, since there's nothing this app can do to repair a file's own header bytes. Reached via a new "Analyze library (cleanup report)…" entry alongside the existing cleanup tool in the Library page's tools menu. A deliberately point-in-time report: the category lists don't live-update as edits are made, matching the existing duplicates/short-tracks tool's own "act on a snapshot, re-run to see fresh results" shape. 26 new tests, all passing after two real issues were found and fixed purely by writing them, not by inspection: (1) in the analyzer itself — a test fixture for a different category's assertion accidentally used identical default title/artist across three tracks, which the *duplicate-tracks* category correctly flagged, revealing the fixture's own mistake rather than a bug in the analyzer; (2) in the widget tests — the exact same sliver-cache-extent-adjacent test-viewport issue this session has now hit repeatedly, this time compounded by `dragUntilVisible`'s final `Scrollable.ensureVisible` animation not being given a `pumpAndSettle()` to actually finish before the next tap, fixed by adding one. 18 tests in `test/library_cleanup_analyzer_test.dart`, 8 in `test/library_cleanup_report_page_test.dart`. | 2026-08-15 | 27 | Plugin platform | Closed item 27's "coarse network permission, declarative-only" gap — specifically the network half; `storage` stays coarse/all-or-nothing, a genuinely separate limitation (`FilesystemPermission.any` has no per-directory scoping to build on the way network already had a ready-made per-host primitive). Before this, declaring `network` in a manifest did nothing at the sandbox layer at all: there was no bridged function that could reach the network, so the permission only ever populated the install-confirmation dialog's text — a plugin literally could not make an HTTP call regardless of what it declared. New `httpGet(url)` bridge function in `plugin_sandbox_bridge.dart` is the first real network capability bridged into the sandbox, and the only way a sandboxed plugin can reach the network at all (it can't import a real Dart HTTP client — the sandbox restricts imports to `package:default`). `PluginRuntime.create` now parses `network:host.example.com`-shaped manifest entries (exactly the spec's own `network:musicbrainz`-style ask) and grants a real `dart_eval` `NetworkPermission.url('https://host.example.com')` per declared host — an off-the-shelf mechanism this session didn't need to build from scratch, unlike `LibraryReadPermission`/`EventsPermission`/`PlaybackControlPermission`, which are custom `Permission` implementations because dart_eval had no equivalent primitive for those domains. `httpGet` checks the *actual requested URL* against whatever was granted via `runtime.assertPermission('network', url)` — a plugin scoped to one host gets a real, catchable denial for any other host, not just a UI-level suggestion. A bare `network` (no host) still grants `NetworkPermission.any`, unrestricted, for backward compatibility with manifests written before scoping existed — `plugins_page.dart`'s `_permissionLabel` now renders the two cases distinctly ("Network access to host.example.com" vs. the broader "Network — can make internet requests to any host"). A non-2xx HTTP response throws rather than returning the body silently, matching this bridge's existing "fail loud enough to notice" stance. 5 new tests in `test/plugin_system_test.dart`'s new `sandbox bridge — httpGet (item 27 network scoping)` group, using `package:http/testing.dart`'s `MockClient` (injected via a new optional `httpClientFactory` parameter threaded through `PluginSandboxBridge`/`PluginRuntime.create`, the same "real component, fake transport" pattern `RadioPlugin`/`OpenSubsonicPlugin` already established) — covering a scoped grant succeeding for its own host, being denied for a different host, a bare grant reaching any host, no grant at all being denied, and a non-2xx response throwing. One genuine finding while writing the last of those, not a bug: a synchronous permission denial (thrown before the guest's `await` ever suspends) gets wrapped as `PluginRuntimeException` by `PluginRuntime.callHook`'s own try/catch, but a real async network failure — which only ever surfaces *after* a genuine suspend, once `callHook`'s synchronous try/catch has already returned control to the caller — comes back as the raw, unwrapped exception instead; a pre-existing `callHook` characteristic common to every async bridge function (not something this increment introduced), newly exercised by a real async failure case for the first time and accounted for in the test rather than touched, to keep this increment's scope to permissions rather than a broader `callHook` error-handling change. `docs/PLUGIN_SECURITY.md` updated in both places it previously described `network` as declarative-only. Full suite: 792 passing (was 787), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change — touches `lib/core/plugin_runtime.dart`/`plugin_sandbox_bridge.dart` but neither `packages/omnis_plugin_api` nor `Omnis-Plugins`, so no cross-repo version bump needed. |

| 2026-08-15 | 2 | Queue | Added the first real test coverage for `ShuffleRepeatPlugin` — real, working, and older than this session, but with zero tests of any kind until now, despite implementing a genuinely non-trivial two-stage de-clustering shuffle algorithm (a round-robin artist interleave, provably free of avoidable adjacent same-artist pairs, followed by a best-effort local-swap pass for same-album adjacency the interleave alone can't account for). Writing real property-based tests — running the algorithm across many trials against a fixed input and asserting an invariant on every trial, not just checking one fixed fixture once — found a genuine bug within the first pass: the swap-repair loop only ever searched *forward* (`j = i + 1` onward) for a safe partner to swap a conflicting track with, so a conflict landing near the end of a shuffled queue could have too few tracks left after it to find one, even when a perfectly safe partner existed earlier in the list. A throwaway diagnostic script (not part of the committed test suite) measured this concretely before touching any code: 32/500 trials still had a residual conflict on a small 6-track fixture, 8/500 even on a generously-buffered 12-track one with 10 unrelated "obviously safe" tracks available — confirming this was a real, structural gap in the search direction, not just an under-resourced test fixture. Fixed by adding a backward search direction (`j` counting down from `i - 2` to `0`, skipping `i - 1` since swapping two already-conflicting neighbors with each other never resolves anything) once the forward search comes up empty. That fix immediately surfaced a second, related bug: `_swapIsSafe` crashed with a `RangeError` accessing `list[i - 1]` — safe under the *original* forward-only caller, which only ever passed `i >= 1`, but no longer safe once the backward search could pass `i == 0`; fixed by bounds-checking that access the same way the function's own `i + 1`/`j + 1` checks already were, rather than special-casing the caller. Re-running the same diagnostic after both fixes measured 0/500 residual conflicts in both fixtures. 18 new tests in `test/shuffle_repeat_plugin_test.dart`: `deClusteredOrder`'s permutation-preservation and no-adjacent-conflict invariants (checked across dozens of trials each, not once), a dominant-artist (>50%) case degrading gracefully rather than crashing, `"Unknown Album"` correctly never counting as a real album match, `toggleShuffle`'s reorder/restore/persist behavior including the empty-queue and no-context-attached degrade paths, the current-track start-index follow-along and its clamp-to-`0` fallback when the track isn't found in the reordered queue, `cycleRepeat`'s off→all→one→off sequence, `cyclePlayMode`'s combined shuffle/repeat state machine in both directions, and `initialize()` restoring persisted state (using the same "a fresh `PluginStorage` starts cold until explicitly warmed" pattern `ratings_plugin_test.dart` already established). `Omnis-Plugins` suite: 263 passing (was 245), tagged `v0.21.0`, pushed. Cross-repo version bump: this repo's `pubspec.yaml` `omnis_plugins` ref bumped from `v0.20.0` to `v0.21.0`, clean-clone resolution re-verified. Main-repo full suite: 828 passing (unchanged — the new tests live entirely in `Omnis-Plugins`), `flutter analyze` clean in both repos. |

| 2026-08-15 | 39, 40 | Discovery | Closed item 40's "no similarity/distance computation anywhere" gap and added item 39's fourth named recommendation algorithm, "Similar Track," in the same increment — the two are the same underlying computation, just different surfaces. New `lib/core/track_similarity.dart`: `similarityScore(a, b)` is a real weighted distance over `BaseTrack`'s already-populated `genres`/`mood`/`bpm`/`key` fields (Jaccard similarity over the genre set; exact case-insensitive match for mood and key; linear BPM falloff to 0 past a 30-BPM difference), with genre weighted highest (deliberate, opt-in metadata) down to key weighted lowest (many unrelated tracks share a key, so it's really just a tie-breaker). Critically, only signals present on **both** tracks contribute to the weighted sum, which is then renormalized against just the weight of those present signals — a track missing e.g. BPM data isn't penalized relative to one that has it, it's judged purely on whatever both sides actually share. When neither track has any comparable signal at all, the score is `0.0`, not `NaN`/a divide-by-zero crash. `findSimilarTracks(seed, library)` ranks a library by that score against a seed (excluding the seed itself by id) and — deliberately — only ever returns tracks that scored strictly above `0.0`, i.e. share at least one real signal; a seed with no analysis/enrichment data at all yields an empty list rather than an arbitrary or misleading ranking of the whole library, the same "don't manufacture a claim the data can't support" stance `QueuePresetPlugin`'s "Forgotten Favorites"/"Rediscover" presets already established for missing history/ratings data. Wired into `library_page.dart` as a new "Play similar" per-track popup-menu action (`_playSimilar`), reusing the exact `setQueue`+`playAt` sequence `_playTrack` already uses; when nothing qualifies, a snackbar suggests running "Analyze audio" or "Look up metadata" first rather than silently doing nothing. 29 new tests in `test/track_similarity_test.dart`, covering each signal in isolation (identical/case-insensitive/partial/disjoint/absent-on-one-side for genre, mood, BPM, and key independently), combined-signal weighting (genre beats key alone, symmetry, a many-combination bounds sweep asserting the score never leaves `[0, 1]`), and `findSimilarTracks`'s self-exclusion/empty-library/no-data-seed/sort-order/zero-score-exclusion/limit/non-mutation behavior — one test-fixture mistake was caught and fixed while writing these (an assertion compared two differently-constructed "empty genre" tracks that weren't actually equivalent inputs), not a bug in the similarity logic itself, which passed clean. Deliberately not a real acoustic-fingerprint/embedding system — that needs genuine signal-processing infrastructure this environment can't build, the same reasoning `metadata_enrichment_plugin.dart`'s own doc comment already gives for not attempting a from-scratch Essentia integration; this is an honestly-scoped feature-distance metric over data the app already has, not a claim of real audio-content matching. `library_page.dart` has no existing widget-test harness at all (a pre-existing gap predating this change, confirmed by checking — only its standalone `buildLibrarySections` function has tests, in `test/library_grouping_test.dart`), so "Play similar" itself is `flutter analyze`-verified only, consistent with every other action already on that page's per-track menu; the underlying similarity logic it calls has full unit coverage. Full suite: 857 passing (was 828), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. | 2026-08-14 | 42 | Discovery | Closed item 42's genuine gap: `SmartPlaylistPlugin` previously was, despite its name, only a flat mood-string substring matcher — no `RuleCondition`/condition-group model existed anywhere. | 2026-08-15 | 2 | Queue | Closed the "Remove duplicates"/"Clear played" gap named in `OMNIS_2_0_MUSICBEE_COMPARISON.md` §23 (confirmed via `grep` beforehand that neither existed anywhere in `lib/` — a genuine 0%, not already-partially-built). New `lib/core/queue_operations.dart`: `QueueOperations.duplicateIndicesToRemove(queue, {currentIndex})` (keeps the first occurrence of each track id, but never flags `currentIndex` itself even if it's a later duplicate, so the action never disturbs what's currently playing) and `QueueOperations.playedIndicesToRemove(queue, currentIndex)` (every index strictly before the current position). | 2026-08-16 | 28 | Plugin health | Closed "no heartbeat for a silently-hung plugin" for external (downloaded, dart_eval-sandboxed) plugins — health records were previously purely reactive, so a plugin gone silently unresponsive between real hook calls (or simply never invoked for a long stretch) looked identical to a healthy one. Scoped to external plugins deliberately: they're the untrusted "might hang" case `sandbox.dart`'s own doc comments are written around, and it's the only path reusing the existing manifest-driven `hooks: [...]` string mechanism without touching `packages/omnis_plugin_api`. New pure `lib/core/plugin_heartbeat_scheduler.dart` (`PluginHeartbeatScheduler.isDue`, structurally identical to `PluginUpdateScheduler.isDue` but its own independent class per this codebase's "one scheduler per concern" convention). Three new `AppSettings` fields: `pluginHeartbeatEnabled` (default `false`, the same opt-in stance every other background-check toggle takes), `pluginHeartbeatIntervalMinutes` (default 15 — minutes, not days like the update/backup checkers, since a hung plugin left undetected for days is a much worse UX), `lastPluginHeartbeatAt`. New `PluginManager.runHeartbeats({timeout})`: loops enabled external plugins declaring a `heartbeat` hook, calls it through the existing `PluginSandbox.run` (so a throw or timeout produces an ordinary `PluginHealthRecord`, automatically feeding the existing 5-failures/5-minutes auto-disable threshold for free); a plugin with no `heartbeat` hook declared is silently skipped — zero behavior change for the entire existing plugin ecosystem until an author opts in. Deliberately awaits `callHook`'s result when it's a `Future` — unlike `onTrackStart`/`_forwardEvent`'s existing fire-and-forget external-hook calls (a slow "listener" plugin shouldn't block dispatch to everyone else), a heartbeat's entire purpose is detecting whether a plugin actually finishes in time, so dropping the returned Future would make the timeout nearly meaningless for anything but a synchronous throw — a real, deliberate divergence from this file's otherwise-consistent fire-and-forget pattern, called out explicitly in its own doc comment. New `PluginManager.maybeRunHeartbeatsAutomatically`, wired into `MainCore.initialize()` alongside the existing backup/update-check/scan calls. `PluginHealthSummary` gained a new `isUnresponsive` field (true when a plugin's *most recent* record's `hook == 'heartbeat'`), surfaced on `PluginHealthPage` with a distinct icon (`Icons.hourglass_disabled`) and an "Unresponsive — " subtitle prefix instead of the generic crash framing. New "Heartbeat monitoring" `SwitchListTile` on `PluginsPage`, right below the existing "Automatic update checks" one. 5 new tests in `test/plugin_heartbeat_scheduler_test.dart`, 3 in a new `isUnresponsive` group in `test/plugin_health_summary_test.dart`, 5 in a new `PluginManager.runHeartbeats` group in `test/plugin_system_test.dart` (real dart_eval plugin fixtures: a working heartbeat produces no record, a synchronously-throwing one produces exactly one tagged `heartbeat`, an *async* throwing one also produces exactly one — proving the await-fix, not just fire-and-forget — a plugin without the hook is never called, a disabled plugin is skipped), 5 in a new `maybeRunHeartbeatsAutomatically` group in `test/plugin_updates_test.dart`, 1 widget test in `test/plugins_page_test.dart`, 2 in `test/plugin_health_page_test.dart`. One dart_eval interpreter limitation hit and worked around while writing these (not a bug in this codebase's own code): an interpreted `async` function with no genuine `await` point inside it throws a `ReturnAsync`/`Completer` type-cast runtime error, and `Duration(seconds: N)` construction inside interpreted code separately hits a `BoxInt` cast error — both are dart_eval's own limitations, worked around by keeping the "no-op success" fixture's heartbeat fully synchronous and proving the await-fix via an async function that throws instead of one that delays. Two genuinely separate, pre-existing test-infrastructure issues also fixed along the way (both root-caused, not just papered over): `test/plugins_page_test.dart`'s pre-existing "a real sandboxed failure shows a summary count..." test had a `dragUntilVisible`-then-`tap` sequence that intermittently missed its hit-test target once this feature's own new toggle pushed the layout down further — fixed by adding an explicit `ensureVisible` + `pump` between the scroll and the tap to settle the exact position first; the new "Heartbeat monitoring" toggle test itself needed the same `dragUntilVisible` (not `ensureVisible`) treatment from the start, since it sits just past the default test viewport's `ListView` sliver cache extent, same as the Plugin Health summary link already documented needing it. Full suite: 1425 passing (was 1404), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. Item 28's remaining named gap: bundled/in-process plugin heartbeat — `MusicPlugin` has no string-keyed hook mechanism to piggyback on the way external plugins' manifest `hooks: [...]` list does, so covering it needs a `packages/omnis_plugin_api` `MusicPlugin.heartbeat()` addition (backward-compatible default `async {}` body) plus the full cross-repo tag/bump/clean-clone-verify cycle — not attempted in this pass. |

New `MainCore.rescanNow()` — an explicit, ungated rescan (unlike `_maybeRunScheduledScan`, never checks `LibraryScanScheduler.isDue`, and lets a real failure propagate instead of swallowing it, since an explicit tap expects to hear about a failure) reusing the existing `_mergeScanIntoLibrary` unchanged. `home_dashboard_page.dart`'s previously-private `_HomeDashboardPageState`/`_openCustomize` became public `HomeDashboardPageState`/`openCustomizeSheet()` specifically so `HomePage` can reach it via a new `GlobalKey<HomeDashboardPageState>` — the smallest change that lets the palette's "Customize home" action open the same sheet and get the same post-close reload the existing AppBar button already triggers, without either page needing to know the other's internals beyond that one public method. `home_page.dart` gained a `_paletteActions(context, core)` map (a sibling to `GlobalKeyboardShortcuts`, not a growth of it — that class's whole contract is playback-only key bindings operating on `AudioEngine` alone, per its own doc comment, while the palette needs `BuildContext`/`MainCore`/the tab index/`AppSettings`, a different action surface entirely, the same "one thing per concern" split this codebase already applies to `AbRepeatController` vs. `AudioEngine`) and a new `CallbackShortcuts` for Ctrl+K/Ctrl+P, gated by the existing `AppSettings.keyboardShortcutsEnabled` toggle for consistency with every other global keyboard shortcut in the app — a judgment call, not silently assumed. No new opt-in `AppSettings` field: unlike `queueContinuationMode`/`autoBackupEnabled`/etc., the palette only ever fires on an explicit keypress, never unattended/background behavior, so the "default-off opt-in for unattended changes" convention doesn't apply here. 8 new tests in `test/command_palette_test.dart` (the pinned 11-command list, `matchCommands`' empty/keyword/no-match/ranking/custom-list behavior), 6 in `test/command_palette_dialog_test.dart` (renders/filters/no-match message/tap-runs-and-closes/missing-action-is-a-no-op/Enter-runs-first-match — the last two needed a `dragUntilVisible` fix for a dialog-height-constrained `ListView` not building its last item until scrolled, same known cause as `plugins_page_test.dart`'s own precedent). `test/home_dashboard_page_test.dart` needed no changes — the rename was a true no-op for it (asserts by rendered text/type, never the private class name). The actual `Ctrl+K` binding wired inside `_HomePageState.build()` itself is intentionally untested — there is no `test/home_page_test.dart` at all (confirmed via `Glob`), the same standing test-infrastructure gap item 2's `_continueQueue`/item 9's "`AudioEngine` lacks an injectable seam" entries already document; called out here rather than skipped silently, verified instead via `flutter analyze` clean plus the full suite staying green. Deliberately **not** used for the 3-band case: `combinedMultiplier` keeps the plugin's exact original hardcoded `0.6/0.4/0.3` formula for `three` rather than routing through the generalized interpolation (a plain 3-point interpolation gives `0.45` at the middle band, not the original `0.4`) — preserving byte-identical audio behavior for the default case mattered more than one shared formula for every band count, verified by a new regression test asserting the two formulas produce identical output. | 2026-08-17 | 38 | Other providers | Closed one more self-hosted provider — "Ampache," narrowing "other self-hosted providers not attempted" (Emby was the only one closed before this). Chosen specifically because it's tractable the same way Emby/Jellyfin/Plex/DLNA/OpenSubsonic already are: self-hostable with a free, versioned, publicly-documented REST/JSON API and a server-admin-generated API key/password, not a closed or paid developer-registration gate the way Apple Music/SoundCloud/Bandcamp/Tidal/Qobuz were already evaluated and judged not tractable for. New `Omnis-Plugins/lib/ampache_plugin.dart` (`AmpachePlugin extends MusicPlugin`), same structural shape as `EmbyPlugin`/`OpenSubsonicPlugin` but a genuinely fourth distinct self-hosted auth shape: a single endpoint (`server/json.server.php`, chosen by an `action=` query parameter, not a per-resource REST path) issues a session token via a `handshake` call, `auth = SHA256(timestamp + SHA256(password))` (timestamp concatenated *after* the already-hashed password) — extracted into its own small pure `AmpachePlugin.computeHandshakeAuth(password, timestampSeconds)`, since it's genuinely new math versus every sibling plugin (Emby's is a header token, OpenSubsonic's is `md5(password + salt)`), verified against a hand-computed expected hash. The formula and general API shape were confirmed via Ampache's own published documentation (ampache.org/api) and cross-checked against an independent web search, not assumed — one exact field-name ambiguity survived that research (Ampache's own docs list a song's title under both `title` and `name`), handled by reading either defensively rather than picking one and hoping. The one genuinely new decode shape versus every existing provider plugin: `artist`/`album` are nested `{id, name}` objects (not Emby's flat `Artists` list or Subsonic's flat `artist` string) and `genre` is an array of `{id, name}` objects, all extracted via a small `_namedReferenceName` helper. `time` is already whole seconds (no `RunTimeTicks`-style 100ns-tick division needed, unlike Emby). `streamUrl`/`coverArt` use the `url`/`art` fields Ampache's own `songs` response already embeds pre-authenticated per song — no hand-built stream URI needed, unlike every sibling plugin. The one deliberate divergence from the Emby/OpenSubsonic retry-trigger shape, called out explicitly in the class doc: Ampache's own API (versions 3 through 6, confirmed via its official error-format documentation) always returns HTTP 200 even on failure — an expired/invalid session is signaled *only* inside the JSON body as `{"error": {"errorCode": ..., "errorMessage": ...}}`, so `_search`'s retry-once-then-give-up check reads the decoded body, not `resp.statusCode`, unlike every other provider plugin here. New `TrackType.ampache` in `packages/omnis_plugin_api/lib/base_track.dart`, following the exact doc-comment template `subsonic`/`jellyfin`/`plex`/`dlna`/`emby` already establish — confirmed additive/non-breaking by checking `BaseTrack.fromJson`'s `TrackType.values.firstWhere((t) => t.name == json['type'])`, which needs no per-value switch to update. Registered in `bundled_plugins.dart` next to `EmbyPlugin` — no ordering/sequential-init constraint applies. 21 new tests in `Omnis-Plugins/test/ampache_plugin_test.dart`: a dedicated `computeHandshakeAuth` group (matches a hand-computed hash; different passwords produce different hashes for the same timestamp) plus the full Emby-template suite adapted for query-parameter-based mock routing instead of path-based (`isConfigured` gating; handshake success/failure incl. the HTTP-200-with-error-body case and a missing-auth-token response; the nested artist/album/genre decode proven with a real fixture; per-entry defensive decoding; the `title`-vs-`name` fallback; the error-body-triggered retry-once-then-give-up, not `401`-triggered like Emby's; blank query/unconfigured/network-error/non-200 all degrade to `[]` without throwing). Zero main-repo change needed beyond the `packages/omnis_plugin_api` addition — confirmed by grepping `lib/` for existing `Emby`/`emby` references and finding only two doc-comment mentions inside code already generic over any non-local `TrackType` (`play_history_store.dart`, `home_dashboard_page.dart`), the same precedent the Emby entry itself established. **This has not been exercised against a real Ampache server** — verification is protocol-level (mocked HTTP client) only, the same standing honesty caveat every peer connectivity plugin here (Emby/Jellyfin/Plex/DLNA/OpenSubsonic) already carries. `Omnis-Plugins` suite: 573 passing (was 552), tagged `v0.42.0`, pushed. | 2026-08-17 | 38 | Other providers | Closed a third self-hosted provider — "Koel" — narrowing "other self-hosted providers (e.g. Koel) not attempted," the exact example the prior Ampache entry named. Koel (github.com/koel/koel) is MIT-licensed, self-hosted, with a published OpenAPI spec and server-admin-created email/password auth — no paid/closed developer gate, the same tractability bar every self-hosted provider closed here has met. New `Omnis-Plugins/lib/koel_plugin.dart` (`KoelPlugin extends MusicPlugin`), a sixth genuinely distinct self-hosted auth shape: a plain `POST /api/me` login with `{email, password}` (email, not a username) returns a short-lived `Bearer` token — closer to a conventional web-app login than any sibling's handshake/salted-token/header-token scheme. Unlike every sibling's stream URL, Koel's own `GET /play/{id}/{transcode}/{bitrate}` takes its auth as an `api-token` *query parameter*, not the `Bearer` header the rest of its API uses — `streamUrl` embeds it directly, the same query-param-token shape `OpenSubsonicPlugin` already established. The request/response shapes were verified directly against Koel's own published OpenAPI spec (github.com/koel/koel/blob/master/api-docs/api.yaml) via three separate targeted fetches, not assumed from a first pass — the first fetch summary claimed Koel had no server-side search at all (implying a "fetch the whole catalog, filter client-side" design, a materially different and more complex shape than every sibling plugin), which a direct re-read of the spec disproved: a real `GET /api/search/songs?q=...` endpoint exists, so this plugin proxies search directly to the server exactly like every sibling. That same spec has a genuine gap of its own, though — the `search/songs` endpoint's own response schema is an empty `responses: {}` in the published YAML, not a documentation shortfall on this project's end — so `_songsToTracks` decodes defensively for either a bare JSON array or a `{"songs": [...]}` wrapper, and each entry for either the plain `Song` shape (`artist_id`/`album_id` only) or the enriched `SongWithAlbumAndArtist` shape (embedded `artist`/`album` `{id, name}` objects) the spec's own schema components separately document — both shapes have dedicated tests. `coverArt` is deliberately left `null`: Koel's own thumbnail endpoint (`GET /api/album/{id}/thumbnail`) needs a *separate* authenticated (`Bearer`-header, not query-param) round-trip per album that returns a `{"thumbnailUrl": ...}` JSON wrapper rather than a directly loadable URL — an extra network call per search result, a materially different cost than every sibling plugin's embedded-or-URL-builder approach, so it's skipped in this pass rather than manufacturing a URL the field can't honestly carry, the same "don't substitute a misleading claim" stance the whole provider-plugin family already takes. New `TrackType.koel` in `packages/omnis_plugin_api/lib/base_track.dart`, following the exact doc-comment template every prior self-hosted value uses. New pure top-level `dailyMixSeed(date)`/`weeklyMixSeed(date)` in `Omnis-Plugins/lib/queue_preset_plugin.dart` — `dailyMixSeed` encodes the calendar date directly; `weeklyMixSeed` buckets by year + day-of-year÷7 (a documented tunable simplification, not calendar-standard ISO 8601 week numbering — no spec names this feature). Same seed on the same day/week → identical shuffle output; the mix silently rotates the moment the date rolls over, with nothing written to `PluginStorage`. Missing both still degrades to empty, the same standing contract every sibling preset takes. The pool is sorted by id before seeding the shuffle, so the pre-shuffle order — and therefore the seeded output — never depends on `Set` iteration order. New thin `_buildDailyMix`/`_buildWeeklyMix` wrappers pass `dailyMixSeed`/`weeklyMixSeed` through to `_buildFamiliarMix`, each with its own `now:` injectable-for-tests parameter, mirroring `_buildNewReleases`'s own `now:` convention. `presets`/`supportedQueries` grew from nine to eleven entries — zero main-repo UI change needed, same precedent New Releases already established (`_MoodsPage`'s `GridView` derives tiles from `IQueueBuilder.supportedQueries` generically). 16 new tests in `Omnis-Plugins/test/queue_preset_plugin_test.dart`: a `dailyMixSeed`/`weeklyMixSeed` pure-function group (same date → same seed; different days differ; same week-bucket days match; a week boundary differs; two separate year-boundary non-collision checks for both functions) and a `Daily Mix / Weekly Mix` group (no providers → empty; favorites-only pools a favorited track plus its artist's catalog; a deleted favorited id is silently dropped; history-only pools top-played tracks; both-registered overlapping pools dedupe to one entry; the 50-item limit; an empty library; case-insensitive dispatch) — plus the existing "presets in order" test updated for the now-eleven-entry list. **One real test-writing mistake caught and fixed before shipping, not just before this summary was written**: an early draft invented `buildDailyMixForTest`/`buildWeeklyMixForTest` — public test-only wrapper methods that don't exist on the plugin and were never part of the plan, an unrequested API-surface expansion. Caught by `flutter analyze` itself (undefined-method errors), and fixed by following the exact precedent the New Releases entry's own tests already established for this same constraint (`buildQueueFor`'s public dispatch has no way to inject a fixed `now`): the day/week-*stability* claim is tested by calling `buildQueueFor` twice in the same test (necessarily the same real calendar day/week, so no injection needed), while "the seed actually varies by date" is proven separately and sufficiently at the pure-function level by the `dailyMixSeed`/`weeklyMixSeed` group, rather than re-proving it through the full plugin stack with invented surface area. `Omnis-Plugins` suite: 606 passing (was 590), tagged `v0.44.0`, pushed. | 2026-08-17 | 2 | Queue | Closed half of item 2's "queue rules/exclusions" gap — "don't repeat artist"/"don't repeat album" — scoped deliberately narrow: "energy/BPM progression" needs an ordering-algorithm/product decision (ramp up? down? peak-then-taper?), the same reason item 39's "Energy Flow" stayed open this session, so it's left as this gap's documented remainder rather than guessed at here. New pure `lib/core/queue_rules.dart`: `QueueRuleConstraints(minArtistGap, minAlbumGap)` (both default `0` = disabled, the standing "opt-in, nothing changes for anyone who never opens the setting" convention) and `applyQueueRules(tracks, constraints, {groupByAlbumArtist, precedingContext})` — a best-effort single forward-swap repair pass over an already-ordered list, not a generator: for each position that conflicts with what's already placed before it (including `precedingContext`, e.g. the live queue's own tail, so a continuation batch or shuffle doesn't repeat what's already about to finish playing), swaps forward to the nearest later position that doesn't conflict. Explicitly **not guaranteed conflict-free** — a small pool dominated by one artist, or a conflict at the very end of the list with nothing left to swap with, can leave a violation in place rather than looping or throwing, the same "don't manufacture a claim the data can't support" honesty stance `queue_continuation.dart` already takes; proven directly by a dedicated non-throwing/non-looping test rather than assumed. Wired into both places queues get auto-populated: `QueueOperations.shuffledRemaining` (repairs the shuffled tail against itself plus the untouched head) and `continuationTracks` (every branch's return path now routes through a shared `_finalize` helper, with the live queue's own tail as `precedingContext`) — deliberately **not** wired into any manual "Play next"/"Add to queue" action, which stays exactly as the user chose it. `AudioEngine.shuffleRemaining` and `MainCore._continueQueue` forward `constraints`/`groupByAlbumArtist` through from two new `AppSettings` bools, `queueRuleAvoidRepeatArtist`/`queueRuleAvoidRepeatAlbum` (both default `false`, mirroring `pluginHeartbeatEnabled`'s persistence idiom). New "Avoid repeating artist"/"Avoid repeating album" `SwitchListTile`s in `PlaybackSettingsPage` right after "Queue continuation," each subtitle explicitly scoping the feature to shuffle/continuation and not manual actions; a matching `_SearchableSetting` entry added to `SettingsPage`. `PlaylistPage._shuffleRemainingQueue` now reads both settings plus `groupArtistsByAlbumArtist` and passes them through to `AudioEngine.shuffleRemaining`. 23 new/updated tests: 18 pure-function tests in new `test/queue_rules_test.dart` (`isActive` boundaries; no-op cases incl. input-list immutability; artist-gap adjacent-conflict resolution; a pool with no conflicts left untouched; `minArtistGap > 1` requiring more separation; an artist-dominated pool proven to leave an unresolved violation without throwing or looping; `groupByAlbumArtist` switching the comparison field; album-gap resolution incl. same-title-different-albumArtist non-conflict; both constraints combined; `precedingContext` blocking a repeat against tracks outside the list being repaired), 2 new tests in `test/queue_operations_test.dart` (default/no-constraints behavior unchanged; an active constraint provably reorders a `Random`-seeded shuffle's tail — the exact raw-vs-repaired pair for that seed pinned by direct probing, the same "pin a seed's real output" convention the file's own pre-existing deterministic-shuffle test already uses), 2 new tests in `test/queue_continuation_test.dart` (`similarArtist` and `sameGenre`, each proving a seeded raw batch's queueTail conflict gets reordered — again pinned against a probed seed's actual output rather than an assumed one). One pre-existing test double needed updating for the `shuffleRemaining` signature change: `test/playlist_page_test.dart`'s `_FakeEngine.shuffleRemaining` gained the same `constraints`/`groupByAlbumArtist` parameters as the real method, delegating to `QueueOperations.shuffledRemaining` identically. Also fixed in the same pass, found via a GitHub CI failure (not reproducible on a Windows dev machine): `AudioEngine.uriFor` treated a Windows drive-letter path as a scheme-less relative URI on Linux/macOS CI runners, because `Uri.file(path)` defaults its `windows` parameter to `Platform.isWindows` — false on those runners — so `C:\Music\song.mp3` (not starting with `/`) parsed as a relative reference instead of `file://`, an environment-dependent bug invisible on the Windows machine this project is normally built on. Fixed with a `_windowsPathPattern` (`^[a-zA-Z]:[\\/]`) that forces `windows: true` for a drive-letter path regardless of host platform, leaving genuine POSIX paths (`/music/...`) untouched. Full suite: 1511 passing (was 1489), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. Item 2's remaining named gaps: energy/BPM progression ordering, and multiple queue sources. |

Item 48's remaining named gaps: "Add to playlist"/"Create mood"/"Toggle visualizer" commands (each needs a "current track" or plugin-state concept the palette doesn't have — see `command_palette.dart`'s own doc comment), and Artists/Albums/Settings coverage in search-everywhere. |
| 2026-08-18 | 39 | Recommendations | Closed UI_SPEC §13/§14's "user-created moods" gap: until now every Moods-page tile came from a fixed `IQueueBuilder.supportedQueries` vocabulary (bundled-plugin preset names like Chill/Focus/Sleep) — there was no way for a user to define their own named mood, confirmed by grep (no "mood" file anywhere in `lib/`, the concept lived entirely inside `MoodsPageState`). New `lib/core/custom_mood.dart`: `CustomMood` (name, OR-matched genres, OR-matched mood tags, a BPM range, a rating floor, an optional "exclude tracks played in the last N days" rule, an optional suggested-time window, a color, and a closed-vocabulary `CustomMoodIcon`) plus `CustomMoodStore` (the same load/save-JSON-file, caller-owns-the-list shape `PlaylistStore` already established, not a `LibraryRepository`-style shared cache — custom moods are only ever read by the Moods page itself). A deliberate, documented scoping decision on §13's "Energy" field: no `energy` value exists anywhere on `BaseTrack` (checked directly), so `CustomMood` folds the spec's "Energy" and "Tempo" fields into the one real numeric signal this codebase actually tracks per track — `bpm` — rather than inventing a second axis with nothing behind it. §13's "Time: 9 PM – 3 AM" field is intentionally never used to filter *tracks* — `CustomMood.isInTimeWindow` only drives a cosmetic "Suggested now" badge on the mood's own tile, matching the spec's own framing of time as "when this mood makes sense to suggest," not a track property. `CustomMood.matches` ANDs every configured criterion (unlike `smart_playlist_rule.dart`'s separate ALL/ANY/NONE `matchType` — a mood is one cohesive vibe, not a general rule builder); an entirely unconfigured mood matches nothing, the same "not yet configured, not everything" contract `SmartPlaylistRule.apply` already established for a rule with zero conditions. New `lib/ui/mood_builder_dialog.dart`'s `MoodBuilderPage`: a full page (matching `ThemeEditorPage`'s own "too many independent fields for a modal dialog" precedent, not a second `AlertDialog`), with genre/mood-tag `FilterChip` wraps sourced from the real library's own genres/mood values, a `RangeSlider` for BPM, a tap-to-set star row for the rating floor, switches for the recently-played exclusion and time window, and reuses the existing `ColorPickerDialog` for color — returns the built `CustomMood` via `Navigator.pop`, the same "dialog returns a value, caller persists it" shape `ColorPickerDialog` already uses one file over, rather than persisting itself. `MoodsPageState` (`lib/ui/home_page.dart`) now shows custom moods in the same grid as the existing preset tiles (distinguished by the user's own color/icon vs. every preset's generic `Icons.mood`), with a new FAB to create one and a per-tile `PopupMenuButton` (Edit/Delete) — the same trailing-menu convention `playlist_page.dart`'s own list tiles already use. New `playCustomMood` builds a queue via `CustomMood.matches` against the loaded library (rating via `IRatingsProvider`, recently-played ids via `IPlayHistoryProvider.recentlyPlayed`), with the same empty-library/empty-result snackbar UX and setQueue+play flow `playMood` already established for preset moods. Deliberately out of scope, named honestly rather than silently dropped: §14's fuller visual list (Background/Artwork/Animation/Gradient/Sound behavior) — each is its own separate system; only Color + Icon were built here. 19 new tests: 16 in `test/custom_mood_test.dart` (pure `matches`/`isInTimeWindow`/JSON-round-trip logic, plus a `CustomMoodStore` save/load/corrupt-file suite using the same `_FakePathProvider` pattern `test/library_store_test.dart` established), 3 in `test/mood_builder_dialog_test.dart` (Save disabled until named, chip-selection round-trips into the saved `CustomMood`, edit prefills every field). No widget test touches `MoodsPage`'s actual play flow — this codebase has a documented, known limitation that `flutter test` cannot safely construct a real `AudioEngine` (see `docs/OMNIS_2_0_MISSED_DEEP_PHASE.md` and `library_page.dart`'s own lack of a widget-test file for precedent); `MoodsPageState`'s wiring changes are verified via `flutter analyze` only, the same established exception. Full suite: 1623 passing (was 1604), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed — no `BaseTrack` schema change. |

| 2026-08-17 | 2 | Queue | Closed a real UI_SPEC §40/§41 gap surfaced by a fresh spec-vs-code audit (comparing `docs/OMNIS_2_0_UI_SPEC.md` against `lib/ui/` directly rather than trusting the stale `docs/OMNIS_2_0_MISSED_DEEP_PHASE.md`, which still claims several things closed earlier this session as missing): `AudioEngine.moveTrack`/`removeTrack`/`playAt` were fully built and tested, but the only UI calling them was a full-page "Queue" entry buried inside the Playlist tab's own smart-lists section — nothing reachable from the mini-player or Now Playing the way the spec describes, confirmed directly (zero "queue" references anywhere in `lib/ui/widgets/mini_player_bar.dart`/`lib/ui/now_playing_page.dart` before this pass). New `lib/ui/widgets/queue_panel.dart`'s `QueuePanel`: a `DraggableScrollableSheet` bottom sheet showing NOW PLAYING pinned at top and NEXT (the rest of the live queue) as a `ReorderableListView` with swipe-to-remove, opened via `QueuePanel.show(context, engine)` — the same `showModalBottomSheet`/`isScrollControlled: true` convention `EqualizerSheet.show`/`LyricEditDialog.show` already established. A real bug caught while writing `_reorder`, not shipped: `AudioEngine.moveTrack(from, to)` already applies Flutter's own reorder-index off-by-one convention internally (via `QueueOperations.reorder`, confirmed by reading `playlist_page.dart`'s own `_reorderQueue`, which passes `ReorderableListView`'s raw `onReorder` values straight through with no adjustment) — an early draft of this panel's `_reorder` re-applied that adjustment a second time (since it also has to offset both indices past the pinned NOW PLAYING row first), which would have silently landed a dragged track one position off. Fixed to offset-then-pass-through, matching the existing convention exactly. Deliberately does **not** include a HISTORY section (also named in the same spec section): `IPlayHistoryProvider.recentlyPlayed()` returns `PlayRecord`s (track id/title/artist/timestamp, not a full `BaseTrack`), so real tappable rows would need a library id-lookup step this lightweight panel doesn't have — `PlaylistPage`'s own existing "Recently Played" smart list already does that resolution and stays the real place to see play history. New `PlayerLayoutData.onOpenQueue` (mirrors `onOpenEqualizer`/`onActivateVisualizer`'s exact "callback lives on the data object, `NowPlayingPage` owns the wiring" shape) plus a new "Queue" button in `PlayerExtrasRow`, next to Equalizer/Visualizer — reachable from every layout that includes that row. `MiniPlayerBar` gained a `queue_music` icon button next to play/pause, always visible above the bottom nav whenever a track is loaded. 9 new tests: 7 in new `test/queue_panel_test.dart` (empty-queue state; NOW PLAYING/NEXT split at the right point incl. already-played tracks excluded; no NEXT section when the queue ends at the current track; tap-to-play/swipe-to-remove/drag-to-reorder each asserted against the *real* translated queue index, not just "some callback fired" — the reorder test specifically catches the double-adjustment bug class described above, using a real long-press-then-move gesture sequence since a plain `timedDrag` never starts a `ReorderableListView` drag at all), 1 in `test/mini_player_bar_test.dart` (the Queue button opens `QueuePanel`, not the Now Playing route), 1 in `test/player_widgets_test.dart` (`PlayerExtrasRow`'s Queue button always renders and fires `onOpenQueue`). `test/declarative_layout_test.dart`/`karaoke_gestures_layout_test.dart`/`player_layouts_test.dart` each needed a stub `onOpenQueue: () {}` added to their existing `PlayerLayoutData` construction sites for the new required field. Full suite: 1601 passing (was 1592), `flutter analyze` clean. Main-repo-only change, no cross-repo bump needed. |
